Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HelloController {
    
    @GetMapping("/hello")
    public String hello() {
        return "Hello, World!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 2: Using @RequestParam

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/greet" and takes a request parameter "name". The method should return a greeting string that says "Hello, {name}!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestParam;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class GreetController {
    
    @GetMapping("/greet")
    public String greet(@RequestParam String name) {
        return "Hello, " + name + "!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 3: Using @PathVariable

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/square/{number}" where {number} is a path variable. The method should return the square of the number.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PathVariable;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class SquareController {
    
    @GetMapping("/square/{number}")
    public int square(@PathVariable int number) {
        return number * number;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 4: Using @RequestBody

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/echo". The method should accept a JSON object in the request body and return the same object. Assume the object is of type `Map<String, Object>`.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestBody;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.util.Map;

@RestController
public class EchoController {
    
    @PostMapping("/echo")
    public Map<String, Object> echo(@RequestBody Map<String, Object> body) {
        return body;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 5: Using @RequestHeader

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/header". The method should read a custom header "X-Custom-Header" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestHeader;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HeaderController {
    
    @GetMapping("/header")
    public String getHeader(@RequestHeader("X-Custom-Header") String customHeader) {
        return customHeader;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 6: Using @CookieValue

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/cookie". The method should read a cookie named "sessionId" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.CookieValue;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class CookieController {
    
    @GetMapping("/cookie")
    public String getCookie(@CookieValue("sessionId") String sessionId) {
        return sessionId;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 7: Using @RequestPart

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/upload". The method should accept a multipart request with a part named "file" which is a `MultipartFile`. The method should return the original filename of the uploaded file.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestPart;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;
import org.springframework.web.multipart.MultipartFile;

@RestController
public class UploadController {
    
    @PostMapping("/upload")
    public String upload(@RequestPart("file") MultipartFile file) {
        return file.getOriginalFilename();
    }
}
```

naïve façade — déjà vu!" (2008) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2009). These works suggest a continued engagement with themes of identity, memory, and cultural narratives.

**2. Analysis of the Evolution of Themes and Styles**

- **Early Career (1990s):** The initial works, such as "The Last of the Mohicans," indicate a focus on historical narratives and perhaps a fascination with the American frontier mythos. The choice of a title referencing a classic novel suggests an engagement with literary heritage and possibly a reinterpretation of historical themes through a contemporary lens.

- **Mid-1990s to Early 2000s:** The shift towards "The Last of the Mohicans" (1995) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (1996) may reflect a deeper exploration of identity and the concept of 'lastness' — perhaps a metaphor for the end of an era or the finality of certain cultural or personal narratives. The recurring use of this title could indicate a thematic preoccupation with the idea of endings or the preservation of cultural memory.

- **Late 2000s:** The works "Naïve façade — déjà vu" (2008) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2009) suggest a maturation in thematic exploration, possibly integrating concepts of perception, memory, and the cyclical nature of cultural narratives. The juxtaposition of 'naïve façade' and 'déjà vu' implies a critical examination of surface-level appearances versus deeper, perhaps subconscious, experiences.

- **Stylistic Evolution:** While specific stylistic details are not provided, the progression from historical narratives to more introspective themes suggests a shift from external storytelling to internal reflection. This could manifest in a move from narrative-driven works to more experimental or conceptual pieces, perhaps incorporating multimedia elements or interactive components.

**3. Hypotheses on Influences and Motivations**

- **Cultural Heritage and Identity:** Given the repeated reference to "The Last of the Mohicans," it's plausible that the artist is influenced by the American frontier mythos and its implications on cultural identity. The repeated use of this title may reflect a personal or collective exploration of heritage, especially within the context of a multicultural society like Singapore.

- **Memory and Perception:** The title "Naïve façade — déjà vu" suggests an interest in how memory and perception shape identity. The artist may be motivated to investigate how individuals construct their identities based on both conscious and subconscious experiences.

- **Socio-Political Context:** Singapore's rapid modernization and its complex cultural tapestry could influence the artist's work, prompting reflections on the tension between tradition and progress, and the preservation of cultural narratives amidst change.

- **Personal Narrative:** The artist's own experiences, perhaps as a member of a minority community or as someone navigating multiple cultural identities, could drive the thematic focus on identity, memory, and cultural narratives.

**4. Proposed Research Plan**

To investigate these hypotheses, the following research plan is proposed:

- **Literature Review:** Compile and analyze existing literature on the artist's works, including exhibition catalogs, critical essays, and interviews. This will provide context and insights into the artist's thematic focus and stylistic evolution.

- **Archival Research:** Access archives of the artist's exhibitions, including photographs, videos, and documentation of installations. This will allow for a comprehensive understanding of the visual and conceptual aspects of the works.

- **Interviews:** Conduct interviews with the artist, curators, critics, and peers to gather firsthand accounts of the artist's motivations, influences, and creative processes.

- **Contextual Analysis:** Examine the socio-cultural environment of Singapore during the periods of the artist's activity, focusing on the dynamics of cultural identity, memory, and heritage. This will help situate the artist's work within broader societal narratives.

- **Comparative Study:** Compare the artist's works with contemporaneous artists in Singapore and internationally who explore similar themes. This will help identify unique aspects of the artist's approach and situate their work within global artistic trends.

- **Theoretical Frameworks:** Apply theories of identity, memory, and cultural heritage to analyze the artist's works. This could involve psychoanalytic theories, postcolonial studies, and cultural studies frameworks.

- **Field Observation:** Attend current exhibitions or installations by the artist, if available, to observe the works in situ and assess their impact on audiences.

- **Publication and Dissemination:** Compile findings into a scholarly article or monograph, contributing to the academic discourse on contemporary Singaporean art and identity narratives.

**Conclusion**

The artist's body of work reflects a nuanced engagement with themes of identity, memory, and cultural narratives. By systematically analyzing their works and situating them within broader socio-cultural contexts, we can gain deeper insights into the motivations and influences shaping their artistic trajectory. The proposed research plan offers a comprehensive approach to exploring these dimensions, contributing to a richer understanding of contemporary art in Singapore.<|endoftext|>

JSON: {"a": 1, "b": [2,3,4]}!.

We need to parse JSON into a map[string]interface{}.

We need to handle errors: if JSON is invalid, return error.

Also, we need to handle the case where the JSON is not a map (e.g., array). In that case, we can return an error.

Implementation:

- Use json.Unmarshal to parse the JSON into a map[string]interface{}.

- If the JSON is not a map, we can return an error.

- For example, if the JSON is an array, we can return an error.

- For the case where the JSON is empty, we can return an empty map.

Now, for the unit tests:

- Test that the function correctly parses a valid JSON string into a map.

- Test that the function returns an error when the JSON string is invalid.

- Test that the function returns an error when the JSON string is not a map (e.g., an array).

- Test that the function correctly handles an empty JSON string.

Now, for the code.

We need to write a Go package that contains the function and the tests.

We can write a package named 'jsonparser', with a function ParseJSONToMap.

We can write the tests in the same package.

Now, for the code.

First, the function:

- ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error)

Implementation:

- Use json.Unmarshal.

- First, we need to unmarshal into a map[string]interface{}.

- But if the JSON is not a map, we need to detect that.

- So, we can unmarshal into interface{}, then check if the result is a map[string]interface{}.

- If not, return an error.

- For empty JSON string, we can return an empty map.

Now, for the tests:

- TestValidJSON: test that a valid JSON string is parsed correctly.

- TestInvalidJSON: test that an invalid JSON string returns an error.

- TestNonMapJSON: test that a JSON string that is not a map (e.g., array) returns an error.

- TestEmptyJSON: test that an empty JSON string returns an empty map.

Now, for the code.

We need to write a Go file with the function and the tests.

Now, let's proceed to write the code.

First, the explanation.

Explanation:

We implement a function ParseJSONToMap that takes a JSON string and parses it into a map[string]interface{}.

The function uses the encoding/json package to unmarshal the JSON string into an interface{} variable.

After unmarshaling, we check whether the resulting value is a map[string]interface{}.

If it is, we return the map.

If not, we return an error indicating that the JSON is not a map.

If the JSON string is empty, we return an empty map.

We also write unit tests to verify the function's behavior in various scenarios:

- Parsing a valid JSON string into a map.

- Handling an invalid JSON string.

- Handling a JSON string that is not a map (e.g., an array).

- Handling an empty JSON string.

Now, the code.

We need to write a Go package with the function and the tests.

We can write a file named jsonparser.go with the function, and a file named jsonparser_test.go with the tests.

Now, let's write the code.

First, jsonparser.go:

```go
package jsonparser

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
)

// ParseJSONToMap parses a JSON string into a map[string]interface{}.
// It returns an error if the JSON is invalid or not a map.
func ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error) {
	if jsonStr == "" {
		// Return an empty map for empty JSON string
		return map[string]interface{}{}, nil
	}

	var data interface{}
	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(jsonStr), &data); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

	// Check if the data is a map[string]interface{}
	m, ok := data.(map[string]interface{})
	if !ok {
		return nil, errors.New("JSON is not a map")
	}

	return m, nil
}
```

Now, jsonparser_test.go:

```go

		"c": map[string]interface{}{
			"d": float64(5),
		},
	}

	result, err := ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("Expected no error, got %v

    for char in special_chars:
        if char in password:
            return False
    return True

def validate_password(password):
    """
    Validates the password against the specified criteria.
    Returns a tuple (is_valid, error_message).
    """
    if len(password) < 8:
        return False, "Password must be at least 8 characters long."
    if not has_uppercase(password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one uppercase letter."
    if not has_lowercase(password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one lowercase letter."
    if not has_digit(password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one digit."
    if not has_special_char(password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one special character."
    return True, ""

def main():
    """
    Main function to run the password validation program.
    """
    while True:
        password = input("Enter a password: ")
        is_valid, error_message = validate_password(password)
        if is_valid:
            print("Password is valid.")
            break
        else:
            print(error_message)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Explanation of the Code:**

- **Helper Functions**: Each helper function checks for a specific criterion:
  - `has_uppercase`: Checks for at least one uppercase letter.
  - `has_lowercase`: Checks for at least one lowercase letter.
  - `has_digit`: Checks for at least one digit.
  - `has_special_char`: Checks for at least one special character from the specified set.

- **validate_password Function**: This function uses the helper functions to validate the password. It returns a tuple indicating whether the password is valid and an error message if it's not.

- **main Function**: This function runs an infinite loop, prompting the user for a password and validating it. If the password is valid, it prints a success message and exits. If not, it prints the specific error message and prompts again.

**Test Cases:**

You can test the program by running it and entering various passwords to see if it correctly validates them according to the specified criteria. For example:

- Input: `Password123!` → Output: `Password is valid.`
- Input: `pass` → Output: `Password must be at least 8 characters long.`
- Input: `Password` → Output: `Password must contain at least one digit.`
- Input: `Password123` → Output: `Password must contain at least one special character.`

This program ensures that the user is guided through the password validation process with clear and specific feedback.<|endoftext|>
你好，世界!！"
```

### 2. 使用 `iconv` 命令

如果你在 Linux 或 macOS 上，可以使用 `iconv` 命令来转换文件编码。下面是一个例子：

```bash
iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt > output.txt
```

这条命令将 `input.txt` 文件从 UTF-8 编码转换为 GBK 编码，并将结果输出到 `output.txt`。

### 3. 使用 Python

如果你想在 Python 脚本中处理文件编码转换，可以使用 `codecs` 模块：

```python
import codecs

# 读取 UTF-8 编码的文件
with codecs.open('input.txt', 'r', 'utf-8') as f:
    content = f.read()

# 写入 GBK 编码的文件
with codecs.open('output.txt', 'w', 'gbk') as f:
    f.write(content)
```

### 4. 使用 Notepad++（Windows）

如果你在 Windows 上，可以使用 Notepad++ 来转换文件编码：

1. 打开文件。
2. 在菜单栏中选择 `编码` -> `转换为 GBK`。
3. 保存文件。

### 5. 使用 VS Code

在 VS Code 中，你可以通过以下步骤转换文件编码：

1. 打开文件。
2. 在右下角的状态栏中，点击当前编码（例如 `UTF-8`）。
3. 选择 `Save with Encoding` -> `GBK`。
4. 保存文件。

### 6. 使用 `iconv` 在 Windows 上

如果你在 Windows 上，可以使用 `iconv` 的 Windows 版本，或者使用 `chcp` 命令来更改控制台的代码页：

```cmd
chcp 936
```

然后使用 `iconv` 或其他工具进行转换。

### 7. 使用 `sed` 或 `awk`（Linux/macOS）

如果你想在命令行中使用 `sed` 或 `awk` 进行转换，可以结合 `iconv`：

```bash
iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt | sed 's/old/new/g' > output.txt
```

### 8. 使用 `awk` 进行编码转换

```bash
awk '{print $0}' input.txt | iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk > output.txt
```

### 9. 使用 `perl`

```perl
use Encode;
open(my $in, "<:encoding(UTF-8)", "input.txt") or die $!;
open(my $out, ">:encoding(GBK)", "output.txt") or die $!;
while (<$in>) {
    print $out $_;
}
close $in;
close $out;
```

### 10. 使用 `sed` 进行编码转换

close $in;
close $out;

В этом примере функция `count_words` принимает строку текста и возвращает количество слов в этом тексте. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

Конечно! Вот пример кода на Python, который подсчитывает количество слов в тексте:

```python
def count_words(text):
    # Разделяем текст на слова, используя пробелы и знаки препинания
    words = text.split()
    # Возвращаем количество слов
    return len(words)

# Пример текста
text = "Привет, мир! Это тестовый текст для проверки функции подсчета слов. В этом тексте есть несколько слов, и мы хотим убедиться, что функция правильно считает их количество. Спасибо за внимание!"

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов
word_count = count_words(text)

# Выводим результат
print(f"Количество слов в тексте: {word_count}")
```

В этом примере функция `count_words` принимает строку текста и возвращает количество слов в этом тексте. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

### شرح الكود:
1. **استيراد المكتبة**: `gTTS` من مكتبة `gtts`.
2. **تحديد النص**: النص الذي تريد تحويله إلى صوت.
3. **إنشاء كائن `gTTS`**: يتم تحديد اللغة (`lang='ar'` للغة العربية) وتحديد ما إذا كان الصوت بطيئًا (`slow=False`).
4. **حفظ الملف الصوتي**: يتم حفظ الملف الناتج باسم `output.mp3`.
5. **طباعة رسالة**: لتأكيد نجاح العملية.

### تشغيل الكود:
- احفظ الكود في ملف Python (مثلاً `text_to_speech.py`).
- شغّل الملف باستخدام الأمر:
  ```bash
  python text_to_speech.py
  ```

بعد تشغيل الكود، ستجد ملف `output.mp3` في نفس مجلد العمل، ويمكنك تشغيله باستخدام أي مشغل صوتي.

Sure! Here's a step-by-step guide to converting your text to audio using Python and the `gTTS` (Google Text-to-Speech) library. This will help you continue with your project.

### Step 1: Install the `gTTS` Library

First, you need to install the `gTTS` library. Open your terminal or command prompt and run:

```bash
pip install gtts
```

### Step 2: Write the Python Script

Create a new Python file (e.g., `text_to_speech.py`) and add the following code:

```python
from gtts import gTTS

# Define the text you want to convert to speech
text = "مرحبا بالعالم! هذا هو النص الذي نريد تحويله إلى صوت. هل يمكنك مساعدتي؟"

# Convert the text to speech
audio = gTTS(text=text, lang='ar', slow=False)

# Save the audio file
audio.save("output.mp3")

print("تم تحويل النص إلى صوت بنجاح!")
```

### Step 3: Run the Script

Run the script by executing the following command in your terminal or command prompt:

```bash
python text_to_speech.py
```

### Step 4: Verify the Output

After running the script, you should see a file named `output.mp3` in the same directory as your script. You can play this file using any media player to hear the spoken text.

### Explanation of the Code

- **Importing the Library**: `from gtts import gTTS` imports the `gTTS` class from the `gtts` library.
- **Defining the Text**: The variable `text` holds the Arabic text you want to convert to speech.
- **Creating the `gTTS` Object**: `gTTS(text=text, lang='ar', slow=False)` creates a `gTTS` object with the specified text, language (`ar` for Arabic), and speed (`slow=False` for normal speed).
- **Saving the Audio File**: `audio.save("output.mp3")` saves the generated audio to a file named `output.mp3`.
- **Printing a Confirmation Message**: `print("تم تحويل النص إلى صوت بنجاح!")` prints a confirmation message indicating that the text has been successfully converted to audio.

### Customizing the Script

If you want to customize the script further, here are a few options:

- **Change the Language**: Replace `'ar'` with another language code (e.g., `'en'` for English).
- **Adjust the Speed**: Set `slow=True` if you want a slower speech rate.
- **Add More Text**: Modify the `text` variable to include more or different content.

### Example with Multiple Texts

If you want to convert multiple texts into separate audio files, you can modify the script as follows:

```python
from gtts import gTTS

texts = [
    "مرحبا بالعالم! هذا هو النص الأول.",
    "هذا هو النص الثاني.",
    "هذا هو النص الثالث."
]

for i, text in enumerate(texts):
    audio = gTTS(text=text, lang='ar', slow=False)
    audio.save(f"output_{i+1}.mp3")

print("تم تحويل جميع النصوص إلى صوت بنجاح!")
```

Write a letter from a cat to its human explaining why it knocks things over.!

Dear Human,

I hope this letter finds you well. I am writing to explain why I have been knocking things over in our home. As a cat, I have a natural instinct to hunt and play with objects. This instinct is a part of my natural behavior and is not a sign of disobedience or disrespect.

I understand that my behavior may be frustrating and inconvenient for you, but please know that it is not intentional. I am simply following my natural instincts and exploring my environment. I am not trying to cause harm or damage to your belongings.

I apologize for any inconvenience or frustration that my behavior may have caused. I will do my best to be more mindful of my actions in the future and to avoid knocking over objects. Thank you for your understanding and patience.

Sincerely,
Your Cat

Invent a new holiday and explain how it’s celebrated.!

**Holiday Name:** **Harmony Day**

**Date:** Celebrated annually on the first Saturday of September.

**Purpose:** Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, diversity, and the collective strength of communities. It aims to promote understanding, cooperation, and mutual respect among people from all walks of life.

**Celebration:**

1. **Community Mural Project:** Communities come together to paint a large mural that represents their shared values and diversity. Each participant contributes a small section, symbolizing the idea that everyone has a role in creating a harmonious society.

2. **Cultural Exchange Fair:** Local businesses and community groups set up stalls showcasing their cultural heritage, food, music, and crafts. This fair encourages people to learn about and appreciate different cultures.

3. **Harmony Walk:** A guided walk through the city or town, where participants share stories of how they have contributed to harmony in their community. This walk is a celebration of the collective efforts that bring people together.

4. **Harmony Concert:** A community concert featuring local musicians and performers. The concert is a celebration of the diversity of music and the shared joy of music.

5. **Harmony Games:** A series of games and activities that promote teamwork and cooperation. These games are designed to bring people together and foster a sense of unity.

6. **Harmony Reflection:** A moment of reflection where participants share their thoughts and feelings about the holiday. This reflection is a reminder of the importance of unity and cooperation.

7. **Harmony Feast:** A community feast where people share food and celebrate the diversity of culinary traditions. This feast is a celebration of the shared joy of food and the shared experience of sharing.

8. **Harmony Awards:** A ceremony where individuals and groups are recognized for their contributions to harmony in their community. These awards celebrate the collective efforts that bring people together.

**Conclusion:** Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, diversity, and the collective strength of communities. It encourages people to come together, share their stories, and celebrate the shared joy of harmony.

In the bustling city of Bakersville, where the aroma of freshly baked goods permeated the air, a unique superhero emerged. This superhero, known as "The Rising Loaf," possessed a singular yet extraordinary power: the ability to bake bread with unparalleled precision and creativity.

The Rising Loaf's backstory began in a humble bakery, where a young baker named Amelia discovered a mysterious yeast that seemed to possess a magical quality. As she experimented with different dough recipes, she realized that this yeast could produce bread with an extraordinary rise, resulting in a loaf that seemed to defy gravity.

Amelia, now known as The Rising Loaf, decided to use her newfound power for the greater good. She would rise to the occasion whenever the city faced challenges, using her baking prowess to create bread that could heal wounds, provide nourishment to the hungry, and even inspire hope in the hearts of those who needed it most.

The Rising Loaf's superpower was not just about baking bread; it was about the rising of hope and resilience. Whenever the city faced adversity, The Rising Loaf would rise, both literally and figuratively, to bring comfort and sustenance to its citizens. The story of The Rising Loaf became a legend, a tale of a superhero who could rise above any challenge, just like a loaf of bread rising to perfection.

In the end, The Rising Loaf's story was a testament to the power of resilience, hope, and the simple yet profound act of baking bread. It was a story that would inspire generations to come, reminding them that even in the face of adversity, there is always a rising, a rising, a rising, and a crust that can bring comfort and nourishment to the world.

Hello!, World!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HelloController {
    
    @GetMapping("/hello")
    public String hello() {
        return "Hello, World!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 2: Using @RequestParam

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/greet" and takes a request parameter "name". The method should return a greeting string that says "Hello, {name}!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestParam;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class GreetController {
    
    @GetMapping("/greet")
    public String greet(@RequestParam String name) {
        return "Hello, " + name + "!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 3: Using @PathVariable

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/square/{number}" where {number} is a path variable. The method should return the square of the number.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PathVariable;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class SquareController {
    
    @GetMapping("/square/{number}")
    public int square(@PathVariable int number) {
        return number * number;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 4: Using @RequestBody

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/echo". The method should accept a JSON object in the request body and return the same object. Assume the object is of type `Map<String, Object>`.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestBody;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.util.Map;

@RestController
public class EchoController {
    
    @PostMapping("/echo")
    public Map<String, Object> echo(@RequestBody Map<String, Object> body) {
        return body;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 5: Using @RequestHeader

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/header". The method should read a custom header "X-Custom-Header" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestHeader;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HeaderController {
    
    @GetMapping("/header")
    public String getHeader(@RequestHeader("X-Custom-Header") String customHeader) {
        return customHeader;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 6: Using @CookieValue

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/cookie". The method should read a cookie named "sessionId" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.CookieValue;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class CookieController {
    
    @GetMapping("/cookie")
    public String getCookie(@CookieValue("sessionId") String sessionId) {
        return sessionId;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 7: Using @RequestPart

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/upload". The method should accept a multipart request with a part named "file" which is a `MultipartFile`. The method should return the original filename of the uploaded file.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestPart;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;
import org.springframework.web.multipart.MultipartFile;

@RestController
public class UploadController {
    
    @PostMapping("/upload")
    public String upload(@RequestPart("file") MultipartFile file) {
        return file.getOriginalFilename();
    }
}
```

**2. Analysis of the Evolution of Themes and Styles**

- **Early Career (1990s):** The initial works, such as "The Last of the Mohicans," indicate a focus on historical narratives and perhaps a fascination with the American frontier mythos. - **Mid-1990s to Early 2000s:** The shift towards "The Last of the Mohicans" (1995) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (1996) may reflect a deeper exploration of identity and the concept of 'lastness' — perhaps a metaphor for the end of an era or the finality of certain cultural or personal narratives. The recurring use of this title could indicate a thematic preoccupation with the idea of endings or the preservation of cultural memory.

- **Late 1990s to Early 2000s:** The works "The Last of the Mohicans" (1999) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2000) suggest a continued interest in the themes of heritage, identity, and perhaps the tension between tradition and modernity. JSON: {"a": 1, "b": [2,3,4]}!.

We need to parse JSON into a map[string]interface{}.

We need to handle errors: if JSON is invalid, return error.

Also, we need to handle the case where the JSON is not a map (e.g., array). In that case, we can return an error.

Implementation:

- Use json.Unmarshal to parse the JSON into a map[string]interface{}.

- If the JSON is not a map, we can return an error.

- For example, if the JSON is an array, we can return an error.

- For the case where the JSON is empty, we can return an empty map.

Now, for the unit tests:

- Test that the function correctly parses a valid JSON string into a map.

- Test that the function returns an error when the JSON string is invalid.

- Test that the function returns an error when the JSON string is not a map (e.g., an array).

- Test that the function correctly handles an empty JSON string.

Now, for the code.

We need to write a Go package that contains the function and the tests.

We can write a package named 'jsonparser', with a function ParseJSONToMap.

We can write the tests in the same package.

Now, for the code.

First, the function:

- ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error)

Implementation:

- Use json.Unmarshal.

- First, we need to unmarshal into a map[string]interface{}.

- But if the JSON is not a map, we need to detect that.

- So, we can unmarshal into an interface{}, then check if the result is a map[string]interface{}.

- If not, return an error.

- For empty JSON string, we can return an empty map.

Now, for the tests:

- TestValidJSON: test that a valid JSON string is parsed correctly.

- TestInvalidJSON: test that an invalid JSON string returns an error.

- TestNonMapJSON: test that a JSON string that is not a map (e.g., array) returns an error.

- TestEmptyJSON: test that an empty JSON string returns an empty map.

Now, for the code.

We need to write a Go file with the function and the tests.

Now, let's proceed to write the code.

First, the explanation.

Explanation:

We implement a function ParseJSONToMap that takes a JSON string and parses it into a map[string]interface{}.

The function uses the encoding/json package to unmarshal the JSON string into an interface{}.

After unmarshaling, we check if the resulting value is a map[string]interface{}.

If it is, we return the map.

If not, we return an error indicating that the JSON is not a map.

If the JSON string is empty, we return an empty map.

We also write unit tests to verify the function's behavior in various scenarios:

- Parsing a valid JSON string into a map.

- Handling an invalid JSON string.

- Handling a JSON string that is not a map (e.g., an array).

- Handling an empty JSON string.

Now, the code.

We need to write a Go package with the function and the tests.

We can write a file named jsonparser.go with the function, and a file named jsonparser_test.go with the tests.

Now, let's write the code.

First, jsonparser.go:

```go
package jsonparser

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
)

// ParseJSONToMap parses a JSON string into a map[string]interface{}.
// It returns an error if the JSON is invalid or not a map.
func ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error) {
	if jsonStr == "" {
		// Return an empty map for empty JSON string
		return map[string]interface{}{}, nil
	}

	var data interface{}
	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(jsonStr), &data); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

	// Check if the data is a map[string]interface{}
	m, ok := data.(map[string]interface{})
	if !ok {
		return nil, errors.New("JSON is not a map")
	}

	return m, nil
}
```

def validate_password(password):
    """
    Validates the password against the specified criteria.
    Returns a tuple (is_valid, error_message).
    """
    if len(password) < 8:
        return False, "Password must be at least 8 characters long."
    if not re.search(r'[A-Z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one uppercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'[a-z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one lowercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'\d', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one digit."
    if not re.search(r'[!@#$%^&*()_+\-=\[\]{}|;:\'",.<>\/?`~]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one special character."
    if contains_whitespace(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain whitespace characters."
    if contains_special_chars(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain special characters."
    return True, ""

def main():
    """
    Main function to run the password validation program.
    """
    while True:
        password = input("Enter a password: ")
        is_valid, error_message = validate_password(password)
        if is_valid:
            print("Password is valid.")
            break
        else:
            print(f"Error: {error_message}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Explanation of the Code:**

- **contains_whitespace(password):** Checks if the password contains any whitespace characters using a regular expression that matches any whitespace character (`\s`).

- **contains_special_chars(password):** Checks if the password contains any special characters from the specified set. It iterates through each character in the password and checks if it is in the set of special characters.

- **validate_password(password):** Validates the password against all the specified criteria. It returns a tuple indicating whether the password is valid and an error message if it is not.

- **main():** The main function that runs the program. It prompts the user to enter a password and uses the `validate_password` function to check its validity. If the password is invalid, it displays the corresponding error message and prompts the user again. If the password is valid, it displays a success message and terminates.

### 2. 使用 `iconv` 命令

如果你在 Linux 或 macOS 上，可以使用 `iconv` 命令来转换文件编码。下面是一个例子：

```bash
iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt > output.txt
```

这条命令将 `input.txt` 文件从 UTF-8 编码转换为 GBK 编码，并将结果输出到 `output.txt`。

### 3. 使用 Python

如果你想在 Python 脚本中处理文件编码转换，可以使用 `codecs` 模块：

```python
import codecs

# 读取原始文件
with codecs.open('input.txt', 'r', 'utf-8') as f:
    content = f.read()

# 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
with codecs.open('output.txt', 'w', 'gbk') as f:
    f.write(content)
```

### 4. 使用文本编辑器

许多文本编辑器（如 Notepad++、Sublime Text、VS Code 等）都支持文件编码转换。你可以在编辑器中打开文件，然后选择“另存为”，在保存对话框中选择目标编码。

### 5. 使用 Java

如果你在 Java 中处理文件编码，可以使用 `InputStreamReader` 和 `OutputStreamWriter`：

```java
import java.io.*;

public class EncodingConverter {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws IOException {
        // 读取原始文件
        BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(new FileInputStream("input.txt"), "UTF-8"));
        // 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
        BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new OutputStreamWriter(new FileOutputStream("output.txt"), "GBK"));

        String line;
        while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
            writer.write(line);
            writer.newLine();
        }

        reader.close();
        writer.close();
    }
}
```

### 6. 使用 C#

在 C# 中，你可以使用 `StreamReader` 和 `StreamWriter`：

```csharp
using System;
using System.IO;
using System.Text;

class Program
{
    static void Main()
    {
        // 读取原始文件
        using (StreamReader reader = new StreamReader("input.txt", Encoding.UTF8))
        {
            // 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
            using (StreamWriter writer = new StreamWriter("output.txt", false, Encoding.GetEncoding("GBK")))
            {
                string line;
                while ((line = reader.ReadLine()) != null)
                {
                    writer.WriteLine(line);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

### 7. 使用 Node.js

在 Node.js 中，你可以使用 `fs` 模块和 `iconv-lite` 库：

```javascript
const fs = require('fs');
const iconv = require('iconv-lite');

// 读取原始文件
const content = fs.readFileSync('input.txt', 'utf8');

// 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
fs.writeFileSync('output.txt', iconv.encode(content, 'gbk'));
```

### 8. 使用 Excel

如果你在 Excel 中处理数据，可以将文件导入 Excel，然后使用“另存为”功能，选择目标编码。

### 9. 使用数据库

如果你在数据库中存储文本数据，可以使用数据库的导出功能，指定目标编码。

### 10. 使用在线工具

有许多在线工具可以帮助你转换文件编码，例如：

- [Convertio](https://convertio.co/zh/)
- [Online-Convert](https://www.online-convert.com/)

这些工具通常支持多种文件格式和编码转换。

### 11. 使用 `sed` 命令

在 Linux 或 macOS 上，你可以使用 `sed` 命令进行简单的文本处理，但不适合编码转换。

### 12. 使用 `awk` 命令

类似于 `sed`，`awk` 也不适合编码转换。

### 13. 使用 `perl` 命令

在 Perl 中，你可以使用 `Encode` 模块：

```perl
use Encode qw(encode decode);

open(my $in, '<:encoding(UTF-8)', 'input.txt') or die $!;
open(my $out, '>:encoding(GBK)', 'output.txt') or die $!;

while (<$in>) {
    print $out $_;
}

close($in);
close($out);
```

### 14. 使用 `ruby` 命令

```ruby
File.open('input.txt', 'r:UTF-8') do |in_file|

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов

# Выводим результат
print(f"Количество слов в тексте: {word_count}")

Конечно! Вот пример кода на Python, который подсчитывает количество слов в тексте:

```python
def count_words(text):
    # Разделяем текст на слова, используя пробелы и знаки препинания
    words = text.split()
    # Возвращаем количество слов
    return len(words)

# Пример текста
text = "Привет, мир! Это тестовый текст для проверки функции подсчета слов. В этом тексте есть несколько слов, и мы хотим убедиться, что функция правильно считает их количество. Спасибо за внимание!"

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов
word_count = count_words(text)

# Выводим результат
print(f"Количество слов в тексте: {word_count}")
```

Этот код разбивает текст на слова, используя пробелы и знаки препинания, и возвращает количество слов. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

    print(f"Original text: {original_text}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded_text}")

def test_empty_string():
    print("\nTest: Empty String")
    original_text = ""
    print(f"Original text: '{original_text}'")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: '{encoded_text}'")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: '{decoded_text}'")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_special_characters():
    print("\nTest: Special Characters")
    original_text = "¡Hola! ¿Cómo estás? 1234567890"
    print(f"Original text: {original_text}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded_text}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: {decoded_text}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_long_text():
    print("\nTest: Long Text")
    original_text = "This is a long text. " * 100  # Repeat to make it long
    print(f"Original text length: {len(original_text)}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text length: {len(encoded_text)}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text length: {len(decoded_text)}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_ascii_text()
    test_non_ascii_text()
    test_empty_string()
    test_special_characters()
    test_long_text()

It seems like you're looking for a way to test the display of smart quotes and single quotes in a text. Here's a simple example of how you might structure such a test in a programming context, such as using Python:

```python
def test_smart_and_single_quotes():
    # Define the test strings
    smart_quotes = "“Smart quotes”"
    single_quotes = "‘Single quotes’"

    # Expected output
    expected_smart_quotes = "“Smart quotes”"
    expected_single_quotes = "‘Single quotes’"

    # Test if the smart quotes are displayed correctly
    assert smart_quotes == expected_smart_quotes, "Smart quotes test failed"

    # Test if the single quotes are displayed correctly
    assert single_quotes == expected_single_quotes, "Single quotes test failed"

    print("All tests passed successfully!")

# Run the test
test_smart_and_single_quotes()
```

In this example, the function `test_smart_and_single_quotes()` checks whether the smart quotes and single quotes are displayed correctly by comparing them to expected values. If the actual output matches the expected output, the test passes; otherwise, it fails.

If you're working in a different programming language or environment, the concept remains the same: define the expected output, compare it to the actual output, and assert that they match.

Dear Human,

I hope this letter finds you well. I am writing to explain why I have been knocking things over in our home. As a cat, I have a natural instinct to hunt and play with objects. This instinct is a part of my natural behavior and is not a sign of disobedience or disrespect.

I understand that my behavior may be frustrating and inconvenient for you, but please know that it is not intentional. I am simply following my natural instincts and exploring my environment. I am not trying to cause harm or damage to your belongings.

I apologize for any inconvenience or frustration that my behavior may have caused. I will do my best to be more mindful of my actions in the future and to avoid knocking over objects. Thank you for your understanding and patience.

Sincerely,
Your Cat

---

Dear Human,

I hope this letter finds you well. I am writing to explain why I have been knocking things over in our home. As a cat, I have a natural instinct to hunt and play with objects. This instinct is a part of my natural behavior and is not a sign of disobedience or disrespect.

I understand that my behavior may be frustrating and inconvenient for you, but please know that it is not intentional. I am simply following my natural instincts and exploring my environment. I am not trying to cause harm or damage to your belongings.

I apologize for any inconvenience or frustration that my behavior may have caused. I will do my best to be more mindful of my actions in the future and to avoid knocking over objects. Thank you for your understanding and patience.

Sincerely,
Your Cat

Invent a new holiday and explain how it’s celebrated.!

**Holiday Name:** **Harmony Day**

**Date:** Celebrated annually on the first Saturday of September.

**Purpose:** Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, peace, and mutual respect among all people, regardless of their backgrounds, cultures, or beliefs. It aims to promote understanding and cooperation across diverse communities, encouraging people to celebrate their differences while recognizing shared values.

**Celebration Activities:**

1. **Community Mural Projects:**
   - Communities come together to paint large murals that depict scenes of unity and shared values. These murals are displayed in public spaces and serve as a visual reminder of the day’s message.

2. **Cultural Exchange Fair:**
   - Local businesses and community groups set up stalls showcasing different cultures through food, music, dance, and crafts. This fair encourages people to learn about and appreciate the diversity within their own community.

3. **Peaceful Protest and Dialogue Sessions:**
   - Organized peaceful protests and dialogues are held in various cities, focusing on issues of social justice, equality, and peace. These sessions are moderated by respected community leaders and aim to foster constructive conversations.

4. **Harmony Walks:**
   - Participants walk through their neighborhoods, engaging in conversations with neighbors and sharing stories about their cultural heritage. These walks are designed to promote understanding and empathy among community members.

5. **Harmony Awards:**
   - Awards are given to individuals, organizations, or businesses that have made significant contributions to promoting harmony and peace within their communities. These awards are presented during a ceremony that highlights the achievements and encourages others to follow suit.

6. **Harmony Music Festival:**
   - A music festival featuring diverse musical styles from around the world. The festival promotes unity through music and encourages people to appreciate the beauty of different musical traditions.

7. **Harmony Workshops:**
   - Workshops are organized to educate people about conflict resolution, empathy, and communication skills. These workshops aim to equip participants with tools to promote harmony in their daily lives.

8. **Harmony Tree Planting:**
   - Communities plant trees together as a symbol of growth, unity, and environmental stewardship. The trees serve as a reminder of the day’s message and the importance of nurturing harmony.

9. **Harmony Storytelling:**
   - Storytelling sessions are organized where people share personal stories of unity, peace, and cooperation. These stories inspire others to embrace harmony in their own lives.

10. **Harmony Digital Campaign:**
    - A digital campaign is launched to spread the message of Harmony Day worldwide. Social media platforms are used to share stories, photos, and videos that promote unity and peace.

**Overall, Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, peace, and mutual respect, encouraging people to embrace diversity and promote harmony in their communities.**

In the bustling city of Crumbville, where the streets were lined with bakeries and the air was always fragrant with the scent of fresh bread, there lived a humble loaf of bread named Yeastie. Yeastie was no ordinary loaf; he possessed a unique superpower that set him apart from all other bread in the city.

Yeastie's power was the ability to rise, rise, and rise. Whenever he was placed in a warm, cozy environment, he would transform into a magnificent loaf of bread, rising to new heights with each passing moment. This power was not just a simple transformation; it was a manifestation of his inner strength and resilience.

The story of Yeastie began when he was a small, unassuming loaf, created by a skilled baker named Mrs. Crumb. Mrs. Crumb had a secret recipe that infused Yeastie with a special ingredient—a rare strain of yeast that possessed extraordinary properties. As Yeastie grew, he discovered that his power was not limited to rising in the oven; it extended to the world around him.

When the city faced a crisis, Yeastie stepped forward as a superhero. He used his rising power to lift the spirits of the citizens, bringing hope and optimism to those who were feeling down. He would rise above the challenges, inspiring others to rise as well. His presence was a reminder that even in the darkest moments, there was always a chance for growth and renewal.

The story of Yeastie was a testament to the power of resilience and the ability to rise above adversity. He became a symbol of hope, a living proof that the crust of life could be a superpower. The city of Crumbville celebrated Yeastie's rise, and his story became a legend that inspired generations to come.

In the end, Yeastie's superpower was not just about rising in the oven; it was about rising in life, rising in hope, and rising in the hearts of those who believed in the power of a loaf of bread.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HelloController {
    
    @GetMapping("/hello")
    public String hello() {
        return "Hello, World!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 2: Using @RequestParam

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/greet" and takes a request parameter "name". The method should return a greeting string that says "Hello, {name}!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestParam;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class GreetController {
    
    @GetMapping("/greet")
    public String greet(@RequestParam String name) {
        return "Hello, " + name + "!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 3: Using @PathVariable

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/square/{number}" where {number} is a path variable. The method should return the square of the number.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PathVariable;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class SquareController {
    
    @GetMapping("/square/{number}")
    public int square(@PathVariable int number) {
        return number * number;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 4: Using @RequestBody

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/echo". The method should accept a JSON object in the request body and return the same object. Assume the object is of type `Map<String, Object>`.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestBody;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.util.Map;

@RestController
public class EchoController {
    
    @PostMapping("/echo")
    public Map<String, Object> echo(@RequestBody Map<String, Object> body) {
        return body;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 5: Using @RequestHeader

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/header". The method should read a custom header "X-Custom-Header" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestHeader;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HeaderController {
    
    @GetMapping("/header")
    public String getHeader(@RequestHeader("X-Custom-Header") String customHeader) {
        return customHeader;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 6: Using @CookieValue

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/cookie". The method should read a cookie named "sessionId" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.CookieValue;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class CookieController {
    
    @GetMapping("/cookie")
    public String getCookie(@CookieValue("sessionId") String sessionId) {
        return sessionId;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 7: Using @RequestPart

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/upload". The method should accept a multipart request with a part named "file" which is a `MultipartFile`. The method should return the original filename of the uploaded file.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestPart;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;
import org.springframework.web.multipart.MultipartFile;

@RestController
public class UploadController {
    
    @PostMapping("/upload")
    public String upload(@RequestPart("file") MultipartFile file) {
        return file.getOriginalFilename();
    }
}
```

**2. Analysis of the Evolution of Themes and Styles**

- **Early Career (1990s):** The initial works, such as "The Last of the Mohicans," indicate a focus on historical narratives and perhaps a fascination with the American frontier mythos. The choice of a title referencing a classic novel suggests an engagement with literary heritage and possibly a reinterpretation of historical themes through a contemporary lens.

- **Mid-1990s to Early 2000s:** The shift towards "The Last of the Mohicans" (1995) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (1996) may reflect a deeper exploration of the American identity and the mythologizing of the frontier. The repeated use of this title could indicate a thematic preoccupation with the erosion of traditional narratives in the face of modernity.

- **Late 1990s to Early 2000s:** The works "The Last of the Mohicans" (1999) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2000) suggest a continued engagement with the themes of cultural identity and the tension between tradition and contemporary society. The inclusion of "The Last of the Mohicans" in the title may serve as a metaphor for the fading of certain cultural narratives or the loss of authenticity in modern contexts.

- **Mid-2000s:** The titles "The Last of the Mohicans" (2005) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2006) may indicate a further deepening of the exploration of identity, perhaps focusing on the personal and collective aspects of cultural heritage. The repeated use of the title could suggest a critical examination of the persistence of cultural myths in contemporary society.

- **Late 2000s:** The works "The Last of the Mohicans" (2008) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2009) may reflect a culmination of the thematic exploration, perhaps culminating in a critical reflection on the role of cultural narratives in shaping identity and the impact of modernity on traditional stories.

**3. Hypotheses on Influences and Motivations**

- **Cultural Heritage and Identity:** The repeated use of "The Last of the Mohicans" as a title suggests a deep engagement with themes of cultural identity, heritage, and the preservation of traditional narratives. The artist may be motivated by a desire to examine how cultural myths and stories influence contemporary identity and how they are affected by modern societal changes.

- **Historical Context:** The choice of a title referencing a classic novel may reflect an intention to critique or reinterpret historical narratives, perhaps challenging the romanticized view of the American frontier and exploring the complexities of cultural interactions.

- **Personal Experience:** The artist's personal experiences, perhaps related to cultural identity or heritage, may have influenced the thematic focus on the preservation and transformation of cultural narratives.

- **Societal Changes:** The artist may be motivated by the rapid changes in society, technology, and cultural dynamics, prompting a reflection on how these changes impact cultural identity and the preservation of traditional narratives.

**4. Proposed Research Plan**

To test these hypotheses, the following research plan is proposed:

- **Literature Review:** Conduct a comprehensive review of existing literature on the artist's works, focusing on critical analyses, interviews, and exhibition catalogs. This will provide insights into the artist's thematic intentions and stylistic evolution.

- **Archival Research:** Examine archival materials, including personal correspondence, sketches, and early drafts, to trace the development of themes and styles over time.

- **Interviews:** Conduct interviews with the artist, if possible, and with contemporaries, curators, and critics who have engaged with the artist's work. These interviews will provide firsthand accounts of the artist's motivations and influences.

- **Contextual Analysis:** Analyze the socio-cultural context during the periods of the artist's active years, focusing on the cultural narratives prevalent in society and how they may have influenced the artist's work.

- **Comparative Analysis:** Compare the artist's works with contemporaneous artists who explored similar themes, to identify common patterns and unique approaches.

- **Thematic Analysis:** Employ qualitative methods to analyze the content of the artist's works, identifying recurring motifs, symbols, and narratives that reflect the artist's engagement with cultural identity and heritage.

- **Publication and Dissemination:** Compile findings into a scholarly article or monograph, contributing to the academic discourse on contemporary art and cultural identity.

**Conclusion**

The artist's body of work, as indicated by the titles and dates, reflects a sustained engagement with themes of cultural identity, heritage, and the impact of modernity on traditional narratives. The evolution of themes and styles suggests a deepening exploration of these concepts over time. The proposed research plan aims to investigate the underlying motivations and influences, providing a comprehensive understanding of the artist's contributions to contemporary art.<|endoftext|>

First, 'jsonparser.go':

package jsonparser

import (
    "encoding/json"
)

func ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error) {
    var result map[string]interface{}
    err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(jsonStr), &result)
    if err != nil {
        return nil, err
    }
    return result, nil
}

Now, 'jsonparser_test.go':

package jsonparser

import (
    "testing"
)

func TestParseJSONToMap_ValidJSON(t *testing.T) {
    jsonStr := `{"a": 1, "b": [2,3,4]}`
    expected := map[string]interface{}{
        "a": float64(1),
        "b": []interface{}{float64(2), float64(3), float64(4)},
    }
    result, err := ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr)
    if err != nil {
        t.Fatalf("Expected no error, got %v", err)
    }
    if !mapsEqual(result, expected) {
        t.Fatalf("Expected %v, got %v", expected, result)
    }
}

func TestParseJSONToMap_InvalidJSON(t *testing.T) {
    jsonStr := `{"a": 1, "b": [2,3,4]` // missing closing brace
    _, err := ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr)
    if err == nil {
        t.Fatalf("Expected error, got nil")
    }
}

func TestParseJSONToMap_NotAMap(t *testing.T) {
    jsonStr := `[1,2,3]`
    _, err := ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr)
    if err == nil {
        t.Fatalf("Expected error, got nil")
    }
}

func TestParseJSONToMap_EmptyJSON(t *testing.T) {
    jsonStr := `{}`
    expected := map[string]interface{}{}
    result, err := ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr)
    if err != nil {
        t.Fatalf("Expected no error, got %v", err)
    }
    if !mapsEqual(result, expected) {
        t.Fatalf("Expected %v, got %v", expected, result)
    }
}

// Helper function to compare two maps
func mapsEqual(a, b map[string]interface{}) bool {
    if len(a) != len(b) {
        return false
    }
    for key, valA := range a {
        valB, ok := b[key]
        if !ok {
            return false
        }
        if !valuesEqual(valA, valB) {
            return false

    for char in special_chars:
        if char in password:
            return False
    return True

def validate_password(password):
    """
    Validates the password against the specified criteria.
    Returns a tuple (is_valid, error_message).
    """
    if len(password) < 8:
        return False, "Password must be at least 8 characters long."
    if not re.search(r'[A-Z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one uppercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'[a-z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one lowercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'\d', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one digit."
    if not re.search(r'[!@#$%^&*()_+\-=\[\]{}|;:\'",.<>\/?`~]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one special character."
    if contains_whitespace(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain whitespace characters."
    if contains_special_chars(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain special characters."
    return True, ""

def main():
    """
    Main function to run the password validation program.
    """
    while True:
        password = input("Enter a password: ")
        is_valid, error_message = validate_password(password)
        if is_valid:
            print("Password is valid.")
            break
        else:
            print(f"Error: {error_message}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Explanation of the Code:**

- **contains_whitespace(password):** Checks if the password contains any whitespace characters using a regular expression that matches any whitespace character (`\s`).

- **contains_special_chars(password):** Checks if the password contains any special characters from the specified set. It iterates through each character in the password and checks if it is in the set of special characters.

- **validate_password(password):** Validates the password against all the specified criteria. It returns a tuple indicating whether the password is valid and an error message if it is not.

- **main():** The main function that runs the program. It prompts the user to enter a password and uses the `validate_password` function to check its validity. If the password is invalid, it displays the corresponding error message and prompts the user again. If the password is valid, it displays a success message and terminates.

**Test Cases:**

You can test the program by running it and entering various passwords to see if it correctly validates them according to the specified criteria. For example:

- Input: `Password123!` → Error: Password must not contain special characters.

### 2. 使用 `iconv` 命令

如果你在 Linux 或 macOS 上，可以使用 `iconv` 命令来转换文件编码。下面是一个例子：

```bash
iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt > output.txt
```

这条命令将 `input.txt` 文件从 UTF-8 编码转换为 GBK 编码，并将结果输出到 `output.txt`。

### 3. 使用 Python

如果你想在 Python 脚本中处理文件编码转换，可以使用 `codecs` 模块：

```python
import codecs

# 读取原始文件
with codecs.open('input.txt', 'r', 'utf-8') as f:
    content = f.read()

# 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
with codecs.open('output.txt', 'w', 'gbk') as f:
    f.write(content)
```

### 4. 使用文本编辑器

许多文本编辑器（如 Notepad++、Sublime Text、VS Code 等）都支持文件编码转换。你可以在编辑器中打开文件，然后选择“另存为”，在保存对话框中选择目标编码。

### 5. 使用 Java

如果你在 Java 中处理文件编码，可以使用 `InputStreamReader` 和 `OutputStreamWriter`：

```java
import java.io.*;

public class EncodingConverter {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws IOException {
        // 读取原始文件
        BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(new FileInputStream("input.txt"), "UTF-8"));
        // 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
        BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new OutputStreamWriter(new FileOutputStream("output.txt"), "GBK"));

        String line;
        while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
            writer.write(line);
            writer.newLine();
        }

        reader.close();
        writer.close();
    }
}
```

### 6. 使用 C#

在 C# 中，你可以使用 `StreamReader` 和 `StreamWriter`：

```csharp
using System;
using System.IO;
using System.Text;

class Program
{
    static void Main()
    {
        // 读取原始文件
        using (StreamReader reader = new StreamReader("input.txt", Encoding.UTF8))
        {
            // 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
            using (StreamWriter writer = new StreamWriter("output.txt", false, Encoding.GetEncoding("GBK")))
            {
                string line;
                while ((line = reader.ReadLine()) != null)
                {
                    writer.WriteLine(line);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

### 7. 使用 Node.js

在 Node.js 中，你可以使用 `fs` 模块和 `iconv-lite` 包：

```javascript
const fs = require('fs');
const iconv = require('iconv-lite');

// 读取原始文件
const content = fs.readFileSync('input.txt', 'utf8');

// 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
fs.writeFileSync('output.txt', iconv.encode(content, 'gbk'));
```

### 8. 使用 Excel

如果你在 Excel 中处理数据，可以将文件导入 Excel，然后使用“另存为”功能，选择目标编码。

### 9. 使用数据库

如果你在数据库中存储文本数据，可以使用数据库的导出功能，指定目标编码。

### 10. 使用在线工具

有许多在线工具可以帮助你转换文件编码，例如：

- [Convertio](https://convertio.co/zh/)
- [Online-Convert](https://www.online-convert.com/)

这些工具通常支持多种文件格式和编码转换。

### 11. 使用 `sed` 命令

在 Linux 或 macOS 上，你可以使用 `sed` 命令进行简单的文本处理，但不适合编码转换。

### 12. 使用 `awk` 命令

类似于 `sed`，`awk` 也不适合编码转换。

### 13. 使用 `perl` 命令

在 Perl 中，你可以使用 `Encode` 模块：

```perl
use Encode qw(encode decode);

open(my $in, '<:encoding(UTF-8)', 'input.txt') or die $!;
open(my $out, '>:encoding(GBK)', 'output.txt') or die $!;

while (<$in>) {
    print $out $_;
}

close($in);
close($out);
```

В этом примере функция `count_words` принимает строку текста и возвращает количество слов в этом тексте. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

Конечно! Вот пример кода на Python, который подсчитывает количество слов в тексте:

```python
def count_words(text):
    # Разделяем текст на слова, используя пробелы и знаки препинания
    words = text.split()
    # Возвращаем количество слов
    return len(words)

# Пример текста
text = "Привет, мир! Это тестовый текст для проверки функции подсчета слов. В этом тексте есть несколько слов, и мы хотим убедиться, что функция правильно считает их количество. Спасибо за внимание!"

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов
word_count = count_words(text)

# Выводим результат
print(f"Количество слов в тексте: {word_count}")
```

Этот код разбивает текст на слова, используя пробелы и знаки препинания, и возвращает количество слов. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

こんにちは世界!！"
    print(f"Original text: {original_text}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded_text}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: {decoded_text}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_encode_decode_empty_string():
    print("\nTest: Encode and Decode Empty String")
    original_text = ""
    print(f"Original text: '{original_text}'")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: '{encoded_text}'")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: '{decoded_text}'")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_encode_decode_special_characters():
    print("\nTest: Encode and Decode Special Characters")
    original_text = "¡Hola! ¿Cómo estás? こんにちは！"
    print(f"Original text: {original_text}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded_text}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: {decoded_text}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_encode_decode_long_text():
    print("\nTest: Encode and Decode Long Text")
    original_text = "This is a long text. " * 100  # Repeat to make it long
    print(f"Original text length: {len(original_text)}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text length: {len(encoded_text)}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text length: {len(decoded_text)}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_encode_decode_ascii()
    test_encode_decode_unicode()
    test_encode_decode_empty_string()
    test_encode_decode_special_characters()
    test_encode_decode_long_text()

Dear Human,

I hope this letter finds you well. I am writing to explain why I have been knocking things over in our home. As a cat, I have a natural instinct to hunt and play with objects. This instinct is a part of my natural behavior and is not a sign of disobedience or disrespect.

I understand that my behavior may be frustrating and inconvenient for you, but please know that it is not intentional. I am simply following my natural instincts and exploring my environment. I am not trying to cause harm or damage to your belongings.

I apologize for any inconvenience or frustration that my behavior may have caused. I will do my best to be more mindful of my actions in the future and to avoid knocking over objects. Thank you for your understanding and patience.

Sincerely,
Your Cat

Invent a new holiday and explain how it’s celebrated.!

**Holiday Name:** **Harmony Day**

**Date:** Celebrated annually on the first Saturday of September.

**Purpose:** Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, peace, and mutual respect among all people, regardless of their backgrounds, cultures, or beliefs. It aims to promote understanding and cooperation across diverse communities, encouraging people to celebrate their differences while recognizing shared values.

**Celebration Details:**

1. **Community Mural Projects:**
   - Communities come together to paint large murals that depict scenes of unity and shared human experiences. These murals are displayed in public spaces and serve as a visual reminder of the day’s message.

2. **Cultural Exchange Fair:**
   - Local businesses and community groups set up stalls showcasing different cultures through food, music, dance, and crafts. This fair encourages people to learn about and appreciate the diversity within their own community.

3. **Peaceful Protest and Dialogue Sessions:**
   - Organized peaceful protests are held to advocate for peace and justice. These are followed by moderated dialogue sessions where participants discuss ways to address local and global issues collaboratively.

4. **Community Service Projects:**
   - Volunteers participate in community service projects such as cleaning parks, helping in shelters, or building community gardens. These activities foster a sense of shared responsibility and cooperation.

5. **Educational Workshops:**
   - Schools and community centers host workshops on topics such as conflict resolution, cultural sensitivity, and environmental stewardship. These workshops aim to educate participants on how to maintain harmony in everyday life.

6. **Harmony Parade:**
   - A parade featuring floats that represent different cultures and community groups. Participants wear traditional attire and carry symbols of peace and unity. The parade culminates in a communal gathering where people share a meal and celebrate together.

7. **Night of Lights:**
   - The evening concludes with a "Night of Lights" where participants light candles or lanterns in a symbolic gesture of hope and unity. This event is often accompanied by a communal singing of songs that promote peace and harmony.

**Impact:**
Harmony Day encourages people to reflect on their shared humanity and to celebrate the diversity that enriches society. By fostering understanding and cooperation, it promotes a more peaceful and harmonious world.

Sure! Here's a step-by-step guide to how to **Create a New Holiday**

Creating a new holiday involves a blend of creativity, community engagement, and strategic planning. Here’s a step-by-step guide to help you develop and launch a new holiday:

### Step 1: Define the Purpose and Theme
- **Identify the core message**: What is the holiday about? Is it celebrating a cultural heritage, promoting a social cause, or commemorating a historical event?
- **Choose a theme**: This could be a specific celebration like "Harmony Day" or a broader concept such as "Global Unity Day."

### Step 2: Choose a Date
- **Select a date**: Pick a day that aligns with the holiday’s theme or significance. Consider avoiding conflicts with existing holidays or major events.
- **Consider seasonal relevance**: For example, a holiday celebrating nature might be best in spring or autumn.

### Step 3: Develop a Name
- **Create a memorable name**: The name should be easy to remember and reflect the holiday’s purpose.
- **Check for uniqueness**: Ensure the name isn’t already in use or trademarked.

### Step 4: Plan Celebrations and Activities
- **Design activities**: Plan events that align with the holiday’s theme, such as parades, festivals, or community service projects.
- **Involve local communities**: Encourage local participation to make the holiday more meaningful and inclusive.

### Step 5: Create Promotional Materials
- **Design a logo**: A visual identity helps promote the holiday.
- **Develop marketing materials**: Create flyers, posters, and social media content to spread awareness.

### Step 6: Engage Media and Influencers
- **Reach out to media outlets**: Pitch the holiday to local and national media to gain coverage.
- **Collaborate with influencers**: Partner with influencers who align with the holiday’s theme to amplify its reach.

### Step 7: Launch the Holiday
- **Organize a launch event**: Host a kickoff event to introduce the holiday to the public.
- **Use social media**: Leverage platforms like Instagram, Twitter, and Facebook to create buzz.

### Step 8: Encourage Participation
- **Offer incentives**: Provide rewards or recognition for participation in holiday activities.
- **Create a community platform**: Build a website or app where people can share their experiences and photos.
