Hello!, World!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HelloController {
    
    @GetMapping("/hello")
    public String hello() {
        return "Hello, World!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 2: Using @RequestParam

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/greet" and takes a request parameter "name". The method should return a greeting string that says "Hello, {name}!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestParam;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class GreetController {
    
    @GetMapping("/greet")
    public String greet(@RequestParam String name) {
        return "Hello, " + name + "!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 3: Using @PathVariable

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/square/{number}" where {number} is a path variable. The method should return the square of the number.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PathVariable;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class SquareController {
    
    @GetMapping("/square/{number}")
    public int square(@PathVariable int number) {
        return number * number;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 4: Using @RequestBody

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/echo". The method should accept a JSON object in the request body and return the same object. Assume the object is of type `Map<String, Object>`.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestBody;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.util.Map;

@RestController
public class EchoController {
    
    @PostMapping("/echo")
    public Map<String, Object> echo(@RequestBody Map<String, Object> body) {
        return body;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 5: Using @RequestHeader

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/header". The method should read a custom header "X-Custom-Header" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestHeader;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HeaderController {
    
    @GetMapping("/header")
    public String getHeader(@RequestHeader("X-Custom-Header") String customHeader) {
        return customHeader;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 6: Using @CookieValue

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/cookie". The method should read a cookie named "sessionId" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.CookieValue;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class CookieController {
    
    @GetMapping("/cookie")
    public String getCookie(@CookieValue("sessionId") String sessionId) {
        return sessionId;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 7: Using @RequestPart

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/upload". The method should accept a multipart request with a part named "file" which is a `MultipartFile`. The method should return the original filename of the uploaded file.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestPart;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;
import org.springframework.web.multipart.MultipartFile;

@RestController
public class UploadController {
    
    @PostMapping("/upload")
    public String upload(@RequestPart("file") MultipartFile file) {
        return file.getOriginalFilename();
    }
}
```

**2. Analysis of the Evolution of Themes and Styles**

- **Early Career (1990s):** The initial works, such as "The Last of the Mohicans," indicate a focus on historical narratives and perhaps a fascination with the American frontier mythos. The choice of a title referencing a classic novel suggests an engagement with literary heritage and possibly a reinterpretation of historical themes through a contemporary lens.

- **Mid-1990s to Early 2000s:** The shift towards "The Last of the Mohicans" (1995) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (1996) may reflect a deeper exploration of identity and the concept of 'lastness' — perhaps a metaphor for the end of an era or the finality of certain cultural or personal narratives. The recurring use of this title could indicate a thematic preoccupation with the idea of endings or the preservation of cultural memory.

- **Late 1990s to Early 2000s:** The works "The Last of the Mohicans" (1999) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2000) suggest a continued interest in the themes of heritage, identity, and perhaps the tension between tradition and modernity. The repeated use of the same title may also point to a series or a thematic exploration across multiple works.

- **Mid-2000s:** The titles "The Last of the Mohicans" (2005) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2006) indicate a sustained engagement with the themes of cultural identity and perhaps a reflection on the changing landscape of American society. The use of the same title across multiple works may suggest a series or a thematic exploration across multiple works.

- **Late 2000s:** The works "Naïve façade — déjà vu" (2008) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2009) suggest a shift towards more introspective themes, perhaps exploring the concept of identity and memory. The title "Naïve façade — déjà vu" indicates a focus on the perception of identity and the repetition of experiences, perhaps reflecting on the cyclical nature of cultural narratives.

**3. Hypotheses on Influences and Motivations**

- **Cultural Heritage and Identity:** The repeated use of "The Last of the Mohicans" as a title suggests a deep engagement with themes of cultural identity, heritage, and the preservation of historical narratives. This may reflect a personal or societal concern with the loss of cultural identity in the face of modernization.

- **Historical Context:** The works may be influenced by the historical context of the late 20th and early 21st centuries, including the post-Cold War era, the rise of globalization, and the increasing awareness of cultural diversity and identity politics.

- **Personal Experiences:** The artist's personal experiences, perhaps related to their own cultural background or personal identity, may have influenced the thematic focus on identity and heritage.

- **Artistic Trends:** The artist may have been influenced by contemporary artistic trends, such as the rise of postmodernism, which often involves questioning narratives, identity, and the role of the artist in society.

**4. Critical Assessment of the Artist's Impact**

- **Contribution to Contemporary Art:** The artist's repeated engagement with themes of identity and heritage suggests a significant contribution to contemporary art, particularly in the context of exploring cultural narratives and the preservation of historical memory.

- **Influence on Contemporary Artists:** The artist's focus on identity and heritage may have influenced contemporary artists who explore similar themes, encouraging a deeper examination of cultural narratives and the role of art in preserving historical memory.

- **Impact on Cultural Discourse:** The artist's work may have contributed to broader cultural discourse, particularly in the context of identity politics and the preservation of cultural heritage in a rapidly changing world.

**Conclusion**

The artist's body of work reflects a sustained engagement with themes of identity, heritage, and cultural narratives. The repeated use of "The Last of the Mohicans" as a title suggests a deep preoccupation with the preservation of cultural memory and the exploration of identity in the face of modernization. The artist's work may have been influenced by personal experiences, cultural heritage, and contemporary artistic trends. Their impact on contemporary art and cultural discourse is significant, contributing to a deeper understanding of identity and heritage in contemporary society.

    for char in special_chars:
        if char in password:
            return False
    return True

def validate_password(password):
    """
    Validates the password against the specified criteria.
    Returns a tuple (is_valid, error_message).
    """
    if len(password) < 8:
        return False, "Password must be at least 8 characters long."
    if not re.search(r'[A-Z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one uppercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'[a-z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one lowercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'\d', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one digit."
    if not re.search(r'[!@#$%^&*()_+\-=\[\]{}|;:\'",.<>\/?`~]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one special character."
    if contains_whitespace(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain whitespace characters."
    if contains_special_chars(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain special characters."
    return True, ""

def main():
    """
    Main function to run the password validation program.
    """
    while True:
        password = input("Enter a password: ")
        is_valid, error_message = validate_password(password)
        if is_valid:
            print("Password is valid.")
            break
        else:
            print(f"Error: {error_message}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Explanation of the Code:**

- **contains_whitespace(password):** Uses a regular expression to check if the password contains any whitespace characters. Returns `True` if any whitespace is found, otherwise `False`.

- **contains_special_chars(password):** Defines a string of special characters and checks if any of these characters are present in the password. Returns `True` if any special character is found, otherwise `False`.

- **validate_password(password):** Checks the password against each criterion in order. If a criterion fails, it returns `False` along with the corresponding error message. If all criteria pass, it returns `True` and an empty string.

- **main():** Continuously prompts the user for a password until a valid one is entered. It uses the `validate_password` function to check the password and prints the appropriate messages.

**Test Cases:**

You can test the program by running it and entering various passwords to see if it correctly identifies valid and invalid passwords based on the specified criteria.

**Note:** The program uses only standard Python libraries and is self-contained. It can be run directly without any external dependencies.<|endoftext|>

### 2. 使用 `iconv` 命令

如果你在 Linux 或 macOS 上，可以使用 `iconv` 命令来转换文件编码。下面是一个例子：

```bash
iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt > output.txt
```

这条命令将 `input.txt` 文件从 UTF-8 编码转换为 GBK 编码，并将结果输出到 `output.txt`。

### 3. 使用 Python

如果你想在 Python 脚本中处理文件编码转换，可以使用 `codecs` 模块：

```python
import codecs

# 读取原始文件
with codecs.open('input.txt', 'r', 'utf-8') as f:
    content = f.read()

# 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
with codecs.open('output.txt', 'w', 'gbk') as f:
    f.write(content)
```

### 4. 使用文本编辑器

许多文本编辑器（如 Notepad++、Sublime Text、VS Code 等）都支持文件编码转换。你可以在编辑器中打开文件，然后选择“另存为”，在保存对话框中选择目标编码。

### 5. 使用 Java

如果你在 Java 中处理文件编码，可以使用 `InputStreamReader` 和 `OutputStreamWriter`：

```java
import java.io.*;

public class EncodingConverter {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws IOException {
        // 读取原始文件
        BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(new FileInputStream("input.txt"), "UTF-8"));
        // 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
        BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new OutputStreamWriter(new FileOutputStream("output.txt"), "GBK"));

        String line;
        while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
            writer.write(line);
            writer.newLine();
        }

        reader.close();
        writer.close();
    }
}
```

### 6. 使用 C#

在 C# 中，你可以使用 `StreamReader` 和 `StreamWriter`：

```csharp
using System;
using System.IO;
using System.Text;

class Program
{
    static void Main()
    {
        // 读取原始文件
        using (StreamReader reader = new StreamReader("input.txt", Encoding.UTF8))
        {
            // 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
            using (StreamWriter writer = new StreamWriter("output.txt", false, Encoding.GetEncoding("GBK")))
            {
                string line;
                while ((line = reader.ReadLine()) != null)
                {
                    writer.WriteLine(line);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

### 7. 使用 Node.js

在 Node.js 中，你可以使用 `fs` 模块和 `iconv-lite` 库：

```javascript
const fs = require('fs');
const iconv = require('iconv-lite');

// 读取原始文件
const content = fs.readFileSync('input.txt', 'utf8');

// 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
fs.writeFileSync('output.txt', iconv.encode(content, 'gbk'));
```

### 8. 使用 Excel

如果你在 Excel 中处理数据，可以将文件导入 Excel，然后使用“另存为”功能，选择目标编码。

### 9. 使用数据库

如果你在数据库中存储文本数据，可以使用数据库的导出功能，指定目标编码。

### 10. 使用在线工具

有许多在线工具可以帮助你转换文件编码，例如：

- [Convertio](https://convertio.co/zh/)
- [Online-Convert](https://www.online-convert.com/)

这些工具通常支持多种文件格式和编码转换。

### 11. 使用 `sed` 命令

在 Linux 或 macOS 上，你可以使用 `sed` 命令进行简单的文本处理，但不适合编码转换。

### 12. 使用 `awk` 命令

类似于 `sed`，`awk` 也不适合编码转换。

### 13. 使用 `perl` 命令

在 Perl 中，你可以使用 `Encode` 模块：

```perl
use Encode qw(encode decode);

open(my $in, '<:encoding(UTF-8)', 'input.txt') or die $!;
open(my $out, '>:encoding(GBK)', 'output.txt') or die $!;

while (<$in>) {
    print $out $_;
}

close($in);
close($out);
```

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов

# Выводим результат

В этом примере функция `count_words` принимает строку текста и возвращает количество слов в этом тексте. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

Конечно! Вот пример кода на Python, который подсчитывает количество слов в тексте:

```python
def count_words(text):
    # Разделяем текст на слова, используя пробелы и знаки препинания
    words = text.split()
    # Возвращаем количество слов
    return len(words)

# Пример текста

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов
word_count = count_words(text)

# Выводим результат
print(f"Количество слов в тексте: {word_count}")
```

В этом примере функция `count_words` принимает строку текста и возвращает количество слов в этом тексте. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

### شرح الكود:
1. **استيراد المكتبة**: `gTTS` من مكتبة `gtts`.
2. **تحديد النص**: النص الذي تريد تحويله إلى صوت.
3. **إنشاء كائن `gTTS`**: يتم تحديد اللغة (`lang='ar'` للغة العربية) وتحديد ما إذا كان الصوت بطيئًا (`slow=False`).
4. **حفظ الملف الصوتي**: يتم حفظ الملف الناتج باسم `output.mp3`.
5. **طباعة رسالة**: لتأكيد نجاح العملية.

### تشغيل الكود:
- احفظ الكود في ملف Python (مثلاً `text_to_speech.py`).
- شغّل الملف باستخدام الأمر:
  ```bash
  python text_to_speech.py
  ```

Sure! Here's a detailed guide on how to create a **"**...

Sure! Here's a detailed guide on how to create a **"Text to Speech"** application using Python. This guide will walk you through setting up your environment, installing necessary libraries, and writing a script to convert text into speech.

## Step 1: Set Up Your Environment

### 1.1 Install Python
Make sure you have Python installed on your system. You can download it from the official website: https://www.python.org/downloads/

### 1.2 Create a Virtual Environment (Optional but Recommended)
Creating a virtual environment helps manage dependencies and avoid conflicts.

```bash
# Create a virtual environment
python -m venv text_to_speech_env

# Activate the virtual environment
# On Windows
text_to_speech_env\Scripts\activate

# On macOS/Linux
source text_to_speech_env/bin/activate
```

## Step 2: Install Required Libraries

### 2.1 Install `gTTS` (Google Text-to-Speech)
`gTTS` is a simple library that uses Google's Text-to-Speech API.

```bash
pip install gtts
```

### 2.2 Install `playsound` (Optional)
If you want to play the generated audio file directly from your script, you can use `playsound`.

```bash
pip install playsound
```

## Step 3: Write the Script

Create a new Python file, e.g., `text_to_speech.py`, and add the following code:

```python
from gtts import gTTS
import os

# Define the text you want to convert to speech
text = "مرحبا بالعالم! هذا هو النص الذي نريد تحويله إلى صوت. هل يمكنك مساعدتي؟"

# Create a gTTS object
audio = gTTS(text=text, lang='ar', slow=False)

# Save the audio file
audio_file = "output.mp3"
audio.save(audio_file)

print(f"تم تحويل النص إلى صوت بنجاح! الملف محفوظ في {audio_file}")

# Optional: Play the audio file
# from playsound import playsound
# playsound(audio_file)
```

### Explanation:
- **Importing Libraries**: `gTTS` for text-to-speech conversion and `os` for file handling.
- **Defining Text**: The Arabic text you want to convert.
- **Creating `gTTS` Object**: Specify the language (`lang='ar'`) and whether the speech should be slow (`slow=False`).
- **Saving the Audio File**: The output is saved as `output.mp3`.
- **Optional Playback**: If you want to play the audio immediately, uncomment the `playsound` lines.

## Step 4: Run the Script

```bash
python text_to_speech.py
```

You should see a message indicating that the text has been successfully converted to speech and the file has been saved.

## Step 5: Play the Audio (Optional)

If you included the `playsound` library and uncommented the playback lines, the audio will play automatically after the script runs. If you prefer to play it manually, you can use any media player to open `output.mp3`.

## Additional Tips

### 1. Handling Different Languages
You can change the `lang` parameter to support other languages. For example:
- English: `lang='en'`
- Spanish: `lang='es'`
- French:

def test_empty_string():
    print("\nTest: Empty String")
    original_text = ""
    print(f"Original text: '{original_text}'")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: '{encoded_text}'")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: '{decoded_text}'")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_special_characters():
    print("\nTest: Special Characters")
    original_text = "¡Hola! ¿Cómo estás? 1234567890"
    print(f"Original text: {original_text}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded_text}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: {decoded_text}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_long_text():
    print("\nTest: Long Text")
    original_text = "This is a long text. " * 100  # Repeat to make it long
    print(f"Original text length: {len(original_text)}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text length: {len(encoded_text)}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text length: {len(decoded_text)}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_ascii_text()
    test_non_ascii_text()
    test_empty_string()
    test_special_characters()
    test_long_text()

```python
def test_quotes(text):
    # Check if smart quotes are present
    if "“" in text and "”" in text:
        print("Smart quotes found.")
    else:
        print("Smart quotes not found.")

    # Check if single quotes are present
    if "'" in text:
        print("Single quotes found.")
    else:
        print("Single quotes not found.")

# Example usage
sample_text = "The “Smart quotes” and ‘single quotes’ test."
test_quotes(sample_text)
```

Write a letter from a cat to its human explaining why it knocks things over.!

Dear Human,

I hope this letter finds you well. I am writing to explain why I have been knocking things over in our home. As a cat, I have a natural instinct to hunt and play with objects. This instinct is a part of my natural behavior and is not a sign of disobedience or disrespect.

I understand that my behavior may be frustrating and inconvenient for you, but please know that it is not intentional. I am simply following my natural instincts and exploring my environment. I am not trying to cause harm or damage to your belongings.

I apologize for any inconvenience or frustration that my behavior may have caused. I will do my best to be more mindful of my actions in the future and to avoid knocking over objects. Thank you for your understanding and patience.

Sincerely,
Your Cat

Invent a new holiday and explain how it’s celebrated.!

**Holiday Name:** **Harmony Day**

**Date:** Celebrated annually on the first Saturday of September.

**Purpose:** Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, diversity, and the collective strength of communities. It aims to promote understanding, cooperation, and mutual respect among people from all walks of life.

**Celebration:**

1. **Community Mural Project:** Communities come together to paint a large mural that represents their shared values and diversity. Each participant contributes a unique element to the mural, symbolizing their individuality and the collective harmony.

2. **Cultural Exchange Fair:** A fair where people showcase their cultural traditions, foods, music, and art. This encourages learning and appreciation of different cultures.

3. **Harmony Walk:** A community walk where participants walk together, holding hands or linking arms, symbolizing unity and solidarity. Along the route, there are stations where people share stories, experiences, and lessons learned about harmony.

4. **Harmony Workshops:** Workshops on conflict resolution, empathy, and communication skills. These workshops aim to equip participants with tools to foster harmony in their daily lives.

5. **Harmony Feast:** A communal feast where everyone brings a dish representing their culture or personal preference. The feast is shared, promoting a sense of community and shared experience.

6. **Harmony Awards:** Recognition of individuals or groups who have made significant contributions to fostering harmony within their community. These awards celebrate acts of kindness, inclusivity, and cooperation.

7. **Harmony Reflection:** A moment of reflection where participants share their thoughts and feelings about the holiday, fostering a deeper understanding of the importance of harmony.

**Impact:** Harmony Day encourages people to celebrate diversity, promote understanding, and foster unity. It creates a sense of belonging and shared purpose, strengthening communities and promoting a harmonious society.

Create a backstory for a superhero whose only power is baking bread.! The superhero is named "Breadth" (pronounced "bread-eth") and is a "superhero" in the city of "Baker's Delight". The story should be a good, **...**

In the bustling city of Baker's Delight, where the aroma of freshly baked goods permeates the air, a unique superhero emerged. His name was Breadth, pronounced "bread-eth," and he possessed a singular yet extraordinary power: the ability to bake bread with unparalleled skill and precision.

Breadth's journey began in the humble kitchen of his childhood home, where he learned the art of baking from his grandmother. As he grew older, he discovered that his passion for baking was not just a hobby but a calling. He realized that his talent could be harnessed for a greater purpose.

One fateful day, a crisis struck Baker's Delight. A powerful storm had caused widespread damage, leaving the city in disarray. The residents were in desperate need of sustenance and comfort. Breadth, recognizing the urgency, stepped forward to help.

With his unique power, Breadth could bake bread that not only nourished the body but also healed the spirit. He used his culinary prowess to create bread that could mend broken hearts, soothe weary souls, and bring hope to those in distress. His bread became a symbol of resilience and unity in the city.

Breadth's reputation grew as he continued to use his baking abilities to aid the community. He became a beacon of hope, a symbol of the power of compassion and creativity. His bread was not just food; it was a source of comfort and a reminder that even in the face of adversity, there is always a way to rise above.

As Breadth's legend spread, he became a beloved figure in Baker's Delight. He was celebrated for his selflessness and dedication to the well-being of the city. His story serves as a reminder that even the most seemingly ordinary talents can be harnessed for extraordinary purposes, and that the power of kindness and creativity can bring about positive change in the world.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HelloController {
    
    @GetMapping("/hello")
    public String hello() {
        return "Hello, World!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 2: Using @RequestParam

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/greet" and takes a request parameter "name". The method should return a greeting string that says "Hello, {name}!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestParam;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class GreetController {
    
    @GetMapping("/greet")
    public String greet(@RequestParam String name) {
        return "Hello, " + name + "!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 3: Using @PathVariable

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/square/{number}" where {number} is a path variable. The method should return the square of the number.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PathVariable;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class SquareController {
    
    @GetMapping("/square/{number}")
    public int square(@PathVariable int number) {
        return number * number;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 4: Using @RequestBody

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/echo". The method should accept a JSON object in the request body and return the same object. Assume the object is of type `Map<String, Object>`.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestBody;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.util.Map;

@RestController
public class EchoController {
    
    @PostMapping("/echo")
    public Map<String, Object> echo(@RequestBody Map<String, Object> body) {
        return body;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 5: Using @RequestHeader

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/header". The method should read a custom header "X-Custom-Header" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestHeader;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HeaderController {
    
    @GetMapping("/header")
    public String getHeader(@RequestHeader("X-Custom-Header") String customHeader) {
        return customHeader;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 6: Using @CookieValue

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/cookie". The method should read a cookie named "sessionId" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.CookieValue;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class CookieController {
    
    @GetMapping("/cookie")
    public String getCookie(@CookieValue("sessionId") String sessionId) {
        return sessionId;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 7: Using @RequestPart

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/upload". The method should accept a multipart request with a part named "file" which is a `MultipartFile`. The method should return the original filename of the uploaded file.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestPart;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;
import org.springframework.web.multipart.MultipartFile;

@RestController
public class UploadController {
    
    @PostMapping("/upload")
    public String upload(@RequestPart("file") MultipartFile file) {
        return file.getOriginalFilename();
    }
}
```

**2. Analysis of the Evolution of Themes and Styles**

- **Early Career (1990s):** The initial works, such as "The Last of the Mohicans," indicate a focus on historical narratives and perhaps a fascination with the American frontier mythos. The choice of a title referencing a classic novel suggests an engagement with literary heritage and possibly a reinterpretation of historical themes through a contemporary lens.

- **Mid-1990s to Early 2000s:** The shift towards "The Last of the Mohicans" (1995) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (1996) may reflect a deeper exploration of identity and the concept of 'lastness' — perhaps a metaphor for the end of an era or the finality of certain cultural or personal narratives. The recurring use of this title could indicate a thematic preoccupation with the idea of endings or the preservation of cultural memory.

- **Late 2000s:** The works "Naïve façade — déjà vu" (2008) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2009) suggest a maturation in thematic exploration, possibly integrating concepts of perception, memory, and the cyclical nature of cultural narratives. The juxtaposition of 'naïve façade' with 'déjà vu' implies a critical examination of surface-level appearances versus deeper, perhaps subconscious, experiences.

- **Stylistic Evolution:** While specific stylistic details are not provided, the progression from historical narratives to more introspective themes suggests a shift from external storytelling to internal reflection. This could manifest in a move from narrative-driven works to more experimental or conceptual pieces, perhaps incorporating multimedia elements or interactive components.

**3. Hypotheses on Influences and Motivations**

- **Cultural Heritage and Identity:** Given the repeated reference to "The Last of the Mohicans," it's plausible that the artist is influenced by the American frontier mythos and its implications on identity and cultural memory. This could reflect a personal or collective exploration of heritage, especially within the context of the artist's background.

- **Memory and Perception:** The title "Naïve façade — déjà vu" suggests an interest in how memory and perception shape our understanding of reality. The artist may be motivated to investigate how past experiences influence present perceptions, perhaps challenging the authenticity of cultural narratives.

- **Historical Contexts:** The artist's works may be influenced by historical events or periods, especially those related to colonialism, indigenous cultures, or the evolution of national identities. This could drive a thematic focus on the remnants of past eras and their impact on contemporary society.

- **Personal Experiences:** The artist's personal journey, including education, exposure to various cultures, and personal reflections, may inform the thematic choices. The recurring motif of 'lastness' could be tied to personal milestones or transitions, such as the end of a particular phase in life or the culmination of a creative endeavor.

**4. Proposed Research Plan**

To validate these hypotheses, a comprehensive research plan will be undertaken, encompassing the following steps:

- **Literature Review:**
  - **Academic Journals:** Search for scholarly articles on the artist's work, focusing on thematic analyses and critical reception.
  - **Exhibition Catalogs:** Examine catalogs from exhibitions featuring the artist's work to understand curatorial perspectives and thematic framing.
  - **Interviews and Artist Statements:** Locate interviews, artist statements, and press releases to gain insight into the artist's own explanations of their work.

- **Archival Research:**
  - **Museum and Gallery Archives:** Access archives from institutions that have exhibited the artist's work to uncover documentation, correspondence, and curatorial notes.
  - **Personal Archives:** If accessible, review the artist's personal archives, including sketches, drafts, and notes, to trace the development of themes and styles.

- **Field Studies:**
  - **Exhibition Visits:** Attend current or past exhibitions of the artist's work to observe the presentation and audience reception.
  - **Artist Interviews:** Conduct interviews with the artist, if possible, to discuss motivations, influences, and thematic intentions.

- **Comparative Analysis:**
  - **Contemporaneous Artists:** Compare the artist's work with that of contemporaries to contextualize thematic choices within broader artistic movements.
  - **Historical Contextualization:** Analyze how historical events and cultural shifts during the artist's active periods may have influenced their work.

- **Methodology:**
  - **Qualitative Analysis:** Employ thematic coding to identify recurring motifs and themes across the artist's oeuvre.
  - **Contextual Analysis:** Situate the artist's work within socio-cultural and historical contexts to understand external influences.
  - **Interdisciplinary Approach:** Integrate perspectives from art history, cultural studies, and psychology to enrich the analysis.

- **Expected Outcomes:**
  - **Comprehensive Understanding:** A nuanced understanding

JSON: {"a": 1, "b": [2,3,4]}!.

We need to parse JSON into a map[string]interface{}.

We need to handle errors: if JSON is invalid, return error.

Also, we need to handle the case where the JSON is not a map (e.g., array). In that case, we can return an error.

Implementation:

- Use json.Unmarshal to parse the JSON into a map[string]interface{}.

- If the JSON is not a map, we can return an error.

- For example, if the JSON is an array, we can return an error.

- For the case where the JSON is empty, we can return an empty map.

Now, for the unit tests:

- Test that the function correctly parses a valid JSON string into a map.

- Test that the function returns an error when the JSON string is invalid.

- Test that the function returns an error when the JSON string is not a map (e.g., an array).

- Test that the function correctly handles an empty JSON string.

Now, for the code.

We need to write a Go package that contains the function and the tests.

We can write a package named 'jsonparser', with a function ParseJSONToMap.

We can write the tests in the same package.

Now, for the code.

First, the function:

- ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error)

Implementation:

- Use json.Unmarshal.

- First, we need to unmarshal into a map[string]interface{}.

- But if the JSON is not a map, we need to detect that.

- So, we can unmarshal into interface{}, then check if the result is a map[string]interface{}.

- If not, return an error.

- For empty JSON string, we can return an empty map.

Now, for the tests:

- TestValidJSON: test that a valid JSON string is parsed correctly.

- TestInvalidJSON: test that an invalid JSON string returns an error.

- TestNonMapJSON: test that a JSON string that is not a map (e.g., array) returns an error.

- TestEmptyJSON: test that an empty JSON string returns an empty map.

Now, for the code.

We need to write a Go file with the function and the tests.

Now, let's proceed to write the code.

First, the explanation.

Explanation:

We implement a function ParseJSONToMap that takes a JSON string and parses it into a map[string]interface{}.

The function uses the encoding/json package to unmarshal the JSON string into an interface{} variable.

After unmarshaling, we check whether the resulting value is a map[string]interface{}.

If it is, we return the map.

If not, we return an error indicating that the JSON is not a map.

If the JSON string is empty, we return an empty map.

We also write unit tests to verify the function's behavior in various scenarios:

- Parsing a valid JSON string into a map.

- Handling an invalid JSON string.

- Handling a JSON string that is not a map (e.g., an array).

- Handling an empty JSON string.

Now, the code.

We need to write a Go package with the function and the tests.

We can write a file named jsonparser.go with the function, and a file named jsonparser_test.go with the tests.

Now, let's write the code.

First, jsonparser.go:

```go
package jsonparser

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
)

// ParseJSONToMap parses a JSON string into a map[string]interface{}.
// It returns an error if the JSON is invalid or not a map.
func ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error) {
	if jsonStr == "" {
		// Return an empty map for empty JSON string
		return map[string]interface{}{}, nil
	}

	var data interface{}
	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(jsonStr), &data); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

	// Check if the data is a map[string]interface{}
	m, ok := data.(map[string]interface{})
	if !ok {
		return nil, errors.New("JSON is not a map")
	}

	return m, nil
}
```

    for char in special_chars:

    return True

def validate_password(password):
    """
    Validates the password against the specified criteria.
    Returns a tuple (is_valid, error_message).
    """
    if len(password) < 8:
        return False, "Password must be at least 8 characters long."
    if not re.search(r'[A-Z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one uppercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'[a-z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one lowercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'\d', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one digit."
    if not re.search(r'[!@#$%^&*()_+\-=\[\]{}|;:\'",.<>\/?`~]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one special character."
    if contains_whitespace(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain whitespace characters."
    if contains_special_chars(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain special characters."
    return True, ""

def main():
    """
    Main function to run the password validation program.
    """
    while True:
        password = input("Enter a password: ")
        is_valid, error_message = validate_password(password)
        if is_valid:
            print("Password is valid.")
            break
        else:
            print(f"Error: {error_message}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Explanation of the Code:**

- **contains_whitespace(password):** Uses a regular expression to check if the password contains any whitespace characters. Returns `True` if whitespace is found, otherwise `False`.

- **contains_special_chars(password):** Defines a string of special characters and checks if any of these characters are present in the password. Returns `True` if any special character is found, otherwise `False`.

- **validate_password(password):** Checks the password against each criterion in order. If a criterion fails, it returns `False` along with the corresponding error message. If all criteria pass, it returns `True` and an empty string.

- **main():** Continuously prompts the user for a password until a valid one is entered. It uses the `validate_password` function to check the password and prints the appropriate messages.

**Test Cases:**

You can test the program by running it and entering various passwords to see if it correctly identifies valid and invalid passwords based on the specified criteria.

**Note:** The program uses only standard Python libraries and is self-contained. It can be run directly without any external dependencies.<|endoftext|>

### 2. 使用 `iconv` 命令

如果你在 Linux 或 macOS 上，可以使用 `iconv` 命令来转换文件编码。下面是一个例子：

```bash
iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt > output.txt
```

这条命令将 `input.txt` 文件从 UTF-8 编码转换为 GBK 编码，并将结果输出到 `output.txt`。

### 3. 使用 Python

如果你想在 Python 脚本中处理文件编码转换，可以使用 `codecs` 模块：

```python
import codecs

# 读取 UTF-8 编码的文件
with codecs.open('input.txt', 'r', 'utf-8') as f:
    content = f.read()

# 写入 GBK 编码的文件
with codecs.open('output.txt', 'w', 'gbk') as f:
    f.write(content)
```

### 4. 使用 Notepad++（Windows）

如果你在 Windows 上，可以使用 Notepad++ 来转换文件编码：

1. 打开文件。
2. 在菜单栏中选择 `编码` -> `转换为 GBK`。
3. 保存文件。

### 5. 使用 VS Code

在 VS Code 中，你可以通过以下步骤转换文件编码：

1. 打开文件。
2. 在右下角的状态栏中，点击当前编码（例如 `UTF-8`）。
3. 选择 `Save with Encoding` -> `GBK`。
4. 保存文件。

### 6. 使用 `iconv` 在 Windows 上

如果你在 Windows 上，可以使用 `iconv` 的 Windows 版本，或者使用 `chcp` 命令来更改控制台的代码页：

```cmd
chcp 936
```

然后使用 `iconv` 或其他工具进行转换。

### 7. 使用 `sed` 或 `awk`（Linux/macOS）

如果你想在命令行中使用 `sed` 或 `awk` 进行转换，可以结合 `iconv`：

```bash
iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt | sed 's/old/new/g' > output.txt
```

### 8. 使用 `awk` 进行编码转换

```bash
awk '{print $0}' input.txt | iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk > output.txt
```

### 9. 使用 `perl`

```perl
use Encode;
open my $in, "<:encoding(UTF-8)", "input.txt" or die $!;
open my $out, ">:encoding(GBK)", "output.txt" or die $!;
while (<$in>) {
    print $out $_;
}
close $in;
close $out;
```

### 10. 使用 `sed` 进行编码转换

```bash
sed 's/old/new/g' input.txt | iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk > output.txt
```

### 11. 使用 `awk` 进行编码转换

```bash

```

}
close $in;
close $out;

iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt > output.txt
```

### 20. 使用 `iconv`

В этом примере функция `count_words` принимает строку текста и возвращает количество слов в этом тексте. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

Конечно! Вот пример кода на Python, который подсчитывает количество слов в тексте:

```python
def count_words(text):
    # Разделяем текст на слова, используя пробелы и знаки препинания
    words = text.split()
    # Возвращаем количество слов
    return len(words)

# Пример текста
text = "Привет, мир! Это тестовый текст для проверки функции подсчета слов. В этом тексте есть несколько слов, и мы хотим убедиться, что функция правильно считает их количество. Спасибо за внимание!"

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов
word_count = count_words(text)

# Выводим результат
print(f"Количество слов в тексте: {word_count}")
```

В этом примере функция `count_words` принимает строку текста и возвращает количество слов в этом тексте. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

    print(f"Original text: {original_text}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded_text}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: {decoded_text}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_encode_decode_empty_string():
    print("\nTest: Encode and Decode Empty String")
    original_text = ""
    print(f"Original text: '{original_text}'")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: '{encoded_text}'")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: '{decoded_text}'")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_encode_decode_special_characters():
    print("\nTest: Encode and Decode Special Characters")
    original_text = "¡Hola! ¿Cómo estás? こんにちは！"
    print(f"Original text: {original_text}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded_text}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text: {decoded_text}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_encode_decode_long_text():
    print("\nTest: Encode and Decode Long Text")
    original_text = "This is a long text. " * 100  # Repeat to make it long
    print(f"Original text length: {len(original_text)}")
    encoded_text = encode_text(original_text)
    print(f"Encoded text length: {len(encoded_text)}")
    decoded_text = decode_text(encoded_text)
    print(f"Decoded text length: {len(decoded_text)}")
    assert decoded_text == original_text, "Decoded text does not match original."

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_encode_decode_ascii()
    test_encode_decode_unicode()
    test_encode_decode_empty_string()
    test_encode_decode_special_characters()
    test_encode_decode_long_text()

```python

Invent a new holiday and explain how it’s celebrated.!

**Holiday Name:** **Harmony Day**

**Date:** Celebrated annually on the first Saturday of September.

**Purpose:** Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, peace, and mutual respect among all people, regardless of their backgrounds, cultures, or beliefs. It aims to promote understanding and cooperation across diverse communities, encouraging people to celebrate their differences while recognizing shared values.

**Celebration Activities:**

1. **Community Mural Projects:**
   - Communities come together to paint large murals that depict scenes of unity and shared values. These murals are displayed in public spaces and serve as a visual reminder of the day’s message.

2. **Cultural Exchange Fair:**
   - Local businesses and community groups set up stalls showcasing different cultures through food, music, dance, and crafts. This fair encourages people to learn about and appreciate the diversity within their own community.

3. **Peaceful Protest and Dialogue Sessions:**
   - Organized peaceful protests are held to advocate for peace and unity. These are followed by moderated dialogue sessions where participants discuss ways to foster harmony in their everyday lives.

4. **Harmony Walks:**
   - Participants walk through their neighborhoods, engaging in conversations with neighbors and sharing stories about their cultural backgrounds. This promotes personal connections and mutual understanding.

5. **Harmony Awards:**
   - Awards are given to individuals or groups who have made significant contributions to promoting peace and unity in their communities. These awards are presented in a ceremony that highlights the importance of community service.

6. **Harmony Music Festival:**
   - A music festival featuring diverse musical styles from around the world. The festival encourages people to appreciate different musical traditions and celebrate the shared joy of music.

7. **Harmony Workshops:**
   - Workshops are organized to teach people about conflict resolution, empathy, and communication skills. These workshops aim to equip participants with tools to promote harmony in their personal and professional lives.

8. **Harmony Tree Planting:**
   - Communities plant trees together as a symbol of growth and unity. The trees serve as a reminder of the day’s message and the importance of nurturing harmony.

9. **Harmony Storytelling:**
   - Storytelling sessions are organized where people share stories about their experiences with unity and peace. These stories inspire others to embrace diversity and promote harmony.

10. **Harmony Digital Campaign:**
    - A digital campaign is launched to spread the message of Harmony Day worldwide. Social media platforms are used to share stories, photos, and videos that celebrate unity and peace.

**Overall, Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, peace, and mutual respect. It encourages people to come together, learn about each other’s cultures, and promote harmony in their communities.**

Write a poem in the style of Shakespeare about pizza.!

**Ode to the Divine Pizza**

**Act I: The Arrival**

*Enter the humble kitchen, a stage of culinary delight, where the dough is kneaded, the sauce is spread, and the cheese is melted.*

**Scene I: The Dough**

*The dough, a humble, humble dough, is kneaded with care, a humble, humble dough, a humble, humble dough.*

**Dough:**
O, ye humble dough, thou art the foundation of this culinary art,
With flour, water, yeast, and salt, thou art the heart of this culinary heart.
Thy texture, soft and supple, doth make the pizza rise,
And in the oven, thou dost become a golden, golden surprise.

**Scene II: The Sauce**

*The sauce, a humble, humble sauce, is spread upon the dough, a humble, humble sauce, a humble, humble sauce.*

**Sauce:**
O, ye humble sauce, thou art the flavor of this culinary art,
With tomatoes, herbs, and spices, thou art the heart of this culinary heart.
Thy flavor, sweet and savory, doth make the pizza taste,
And in the oven, thou dost become a golden, golden taste.

**Scene III: The Cheese**

*The cheese, a humble, humble cheese, is melted upon the sauce, a humble, humble cheese, a humble, humble cheese.*

**Cheese:**

Create a backstory for a superhero whose only power is baking bread.! The superhero is named "Breadth" (pronounced "bread-eth") and is a "superhero" in the city of "Baker's Delight". The story should be a good, **...**

In the bustling city of Baker's Delight, where the aroma of freshly baked goods permeates the air, a unique superhero emerged. His name was Breadth, pronounced "bread-eth," and he possessed a singular yet extraordinary power: the ability to bake bread with unparalleled skill and precision.

Breadth's journey began in the humble kitchen of his childhood home, where he learned the art of baking from his grandmother. As he grew older, he discovered that his passion for baking was not just a hobby but a calling. He realized that his talent could be harnessed for a greater purpose.

One fateful day, a crisis struck Baker's Delight. A powerful storm had caused widespread damage, leaving the city in disarray. The residents were in desperate need of sustenance and comfort. Breadth, recognizing the urgency, stepped forward to help.

With his unique power, Breadth could bake bread that not only nourished the body but also healed the spirit. He used his culinary prowess to create bread that could mend broken hearts, soothe weary souls, and bring hope to those in distress. His bread became a symbol of resilience and unity in the city.

Breadth's reputation grew as he continued to use his baking abilities to aid the community. He became a beacon of hope, a symbol of the power of compassion and creativity. His bread was not just food; it was a source of comfort and a reminder that even in the face of adversity, there is always a way to rise above.

As Breadth's legend spread, he became a beloved figure in Baker's Delight. He was celebrated for his selflessness and dedication to the well-being of the city. His story serves as a reminder that even the most seemingly ordinary talents can be harnessed for extraordinary purposes, and that the power of kindness and creativity can bring about positive change in the world.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HelloController {
    
    @GetMapping("/hello")
    public String hello() {
        return "Hello, World!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 2: Using @RequestParam

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/greet" and takes a request parameter "name". The method should return a greeting string that says "Hello, {name}!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestParam;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class GreetController {
    
    @GetMapping("/greet")
    public String greet(@RequestParam String name) {
        return "Hello, " + name + "!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 3: Using @PathVariable

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/square/{number}" where {number} is a path variable. The method should return the square of the number.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PathVariable;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class SquareController {
    
    @GetMapping("/square/{number}")
    public int square(@PathVariable int number) {
        return number * number;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 4: Using @RequestBody

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/echo". The method should accept a JSON object in the request body and return the same object. Assume the object is of type `Map<String, Object>`.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestBody;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.util.Map;

@RestController
public class EchoController {
    
    @PostMapping("/echo")
    public Map<String, Object> echo(@RequestBody Map<String, Object> body) {
        return body;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 5: Using @RequestHeader

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/header". The method should read a custom header "X-Custom-Header" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestHeader;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HeaderController {
    
    @GetMapping("/header")
    public String getHeader(@RequestHeader("X-Custom-Header") String customHeader) {
        return customHeader;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 6: Using @CookieValue

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles GET requests to "/cookie". The method should read a cookie named "sessionId" and return its value.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.CookieValue;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class CookieController {
    
    @GetMapping("/cookie")
    public String getCookie(@CookieValue("sessionId") String sessionId) {
        return sessionId;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 7: Using @RequestPart

Question: In a Spring Boot controller, write a method that handles POST requests to "/upload". The method should accept a multipart request with a part named "file" which is a `MultipartFile`. The method should return the original filename of the uploaded file.

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestPart;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;
import org.springframework.web.multipart.MultipartFile;

@RestController
public class UploadController {
    
    @PostMapping("/upload")
    public String upload(@RequestPart("file") MultipartFile file) {
        return file.getOriginalFilename();
    }
}
```

**2. Analysis of the Evolution of Themes and Styles**

- **Early Career (1990s):** The initial works, such as "The Last of the Mohicans," indicate a focus on historical narratives and perhaps a fascination with the American frontier mythos. The choice of a title referencing a classic novel suggests an engagement with literary heritage and possibly a reinterpretation of historical themes through a contemporary lens.

- **Mid-1990s to Early 2000s:** The shift towards "The Last of the Mohicans" (1995) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (1996) may reflect a deeper exploration of the American identity and the mythologizing of the frontier. The repeated use of this title could indicate a thematic preoccupation with the decline of traditional narratives and the search for new cultural identities.

- **Late 1990s to Early 2000s:** The works "The Last of the Mohicans" (1999) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2000) suggest a continued engagement with the themes of cultural heritage and identity. - **Mid-2000s:** The works "The Last of the Mohicans" (2005) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2006) indicate a possible shift towards more personal narratives, perhaps focusing on individual identity and the search for meaning in a rapidly changing world.

- **Late 2000s:** The works "The Last of the Mohicans" (2008) and "The Last of the Mohicans" (2009) suggest a continued exploration of identity and cultural narratives, perhaps with a more nuanced approach to the complexities of contemporary society.

- **Personal Experiences:** The artist's personal experiences, such as growing up in a multicultural environment or experiencing significant life events, may have influenced the themes of identity and cultural narratives in their work.

JSON: {"a": 1, "b": [2,3,4]}!.

We need to parse JSON into a map[string]interface{}.

We need to handle errors: if JSON is invalid, return error.

Also, we need to handle the case where the JSON is not a map (e.g., array). In that case, we can return an error.

Implementation:

- Use json.Unmarshal to parse the JSON into a map[string]interface{}.

- If the JSON is not a map, we can return an error.

- For example, if the JSON is an array, we can return an error.

- For the case where the JSON is empty, we can return an empty map.

Now, for the unit tests:

- Test that the function correctly parses a valid JSON string into a map.

- Test that the function returns an error when the JSON string is invalid.

- Test that the function returns an error when the JSON string is not a map (e.g., an array).

- Test that the function correctly handles an empty JSON string.

Now, for the code.

We need to write a Go package that contains the function and the tests.

We can write a package named 'jsonparser', with a function ParseJSONToMap.

We can write the tests in the same package.

Now, for the code.

First, the function:

- ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error)

Implementation:

- Use json.Unmarshal.

- First, we need to unmarshal into a map[string]interface{}.

- But if the JSON is not a map, we need to detect that.

- So, we can unmarshal into an interface{}, then check if the result is a map[string]interface{}.

- If not, return an error.

- For empty JSON string, we can return an empty map.

Now, for the tests:

- TestValidJSON: test that a valid JSON string is parsed correctly.

- TestInvalidJSON: test that an invalid JSON string returns an error.

- TestNonMapJSON: test that a JSON string that is not a map (e.g., array) returns an error.

- TestEmptyJSON: test that an empty JSON string returns an empty map.

Now, for the code.

We need to write a Go file with the function and the tests.

Now, let's proceed to write the code.

First, the explanation.

Explanation:

We implement a function ParseJSONToMap that takes a JSON string and parses it into a map[string]interface{}.

The function uses the encoding/json package to unmarshal the JSON string into an interface{}.

After unmarshaling, we check if the resulting value is a map[string]interface{}.

If it is, we return the map.

If not, we return an error indicating that the JSON is not a map.

If the JSON string is empty, we return an empty map.

We also write unit tests to verify the function's behavior in various scenarios:

- Parsing a valid JSON string into a map.

- Handling an invalid JSON string.

- Handling a JSON string that is not a map (e.g., an array).

- Handling an empty JSON string.

Now, the code.

We need to write a Go package with the function and the tests.

We can write a file named jsonparser.go with the function, and a file named jsonparser_test.go with the tests.

Now, let's write the code.

First, jsonparser.go:

```go
package jsonparser

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
)

// ParseJSONToMap parses a JSON string into a map[string]interface{}.
// It returns an error if the JSON is invalid or not a map.
func ParseJSONToMap(jsonStr string) (map[string]interface{}, error) {
	if jsonStr == "" {
		// Return an empty map for empty JSON string
		return map[string]interface{}{}, nil
	}

	var data interface{}
	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(jsonStr), &data); err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

	// Check if the data is a map[string]interface{}
	m, ok := data.(map[string]interface{})
	if !ok {
		return nil, errors.New("JSON is not a map")
	}

	return m, nil
}
```

Now, jsonparser_test.go:

```go
package jsonparser

import (
	"reflect"
	"testing"
)

func TestParseJSONToMap_ValidJSON(t *testing.T) {
	jsonStr := `{"a": 1, "b": [2,3,4], "c": {"d": 5}}`

def validate_password(password):
    """
    Validates the password against the specified criteria.
    Returns a tuple (is_valid, error_message).
    """
    if len(password) < 8:
        return False, "Password must be at least 8 characters long."
    if not re.search(r'[A-Z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one uppercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'[a-z]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one lowercase letter."
    if not re.search(r'\d', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one digit."
    if not re.search(r'[!@#$%^&*()_+\-=\[\]{}|;:\'",.<>\/?`~]', password):
        return False, "Password must contain at least one special character."
    if contains_whitespace(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain whitespace characters."
    if contains_special_chars(password):
        return False, "Password must not contain special characters."
    return True, ""

def main():
    """
    Main function to run the password validation program.
    """
    while True:
        password = input("Enter a password: ")
        is_valid, error_message = validate_password(password)
        if is_valid:
            print("Password is valid.")
            break
        else:
            print(f"Error: {error_message}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Explanation of the Code:**

- **contains_whitespace(password):** Checks if the password contains any whitespace characters using a regular expression that matches any whitespace character (`\s`).

- **contains_special_chars(password):** Checks if the password contains any special characters from the specified set. It iterates through each character in the password and checks if it is in the set of special characters.

- **validate_password(password):** Validates the password against all the specified criteria. It returns a tuple indicating whether the password is valid and an error message if it is not.

- **main():** The main function that runs the program. It prompts the user to enter a password and uses the `validate_password` function to check its validity. If the password is invalid, it displays the corresponding error message and prompts the user again. If the password is valid, it displays a success message and terminates.

### 2. 使用 `iconv` 命令

如果你在 Linux 或 macOS 上，可以使用 `iconv` 命令来转换文件编码。下面是一个例子：

```bash
iconv -f utf-8 -t gbk input.txt > output.txt
```

这条命令将 `input.txt` 文件从 UTF-8 编码转换为 GBK 编码，并将结果输出到 `output.txt`。

### 3. 使用 Python

如果你想在 Python 脚本中处理文件编码转换，可以使用 `codecs` 模块：

```python
import codecs

# 读取原始文件
with codecs.open('input.txt', 'r', 'utf-8') as f:
    content = f.read()

# 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
with codecs.open('output.txt', 'w', 'gbk') as f:
    f.write(content)
```

### 4. 使用文本编辑器

许多文本编辑器（如 Notepad++、Sublime Text、VS Code 等）都支持文件编码转换。你可以在编辑器中打开文件，然后选择“另存为”，在保存对话框中选择目标编码。

### 5. 使用 Java

如果你在 Java 中处理文件编码，可以使用 `InputStreamReader` 和 `OutputStreamWriter`：

```java
import java.io.*;

public class EncodingConverter {
    public static void main(String[] args) throws IOException {
        // 读取原始文件
        BufferedReader reader = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(new FileInputStream("input.txt"), "UTF-8"));
        // 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
        BufferedWriter writer = new BufferedWriter(new OutputStreamWriter(new FileOutputStream("output.txt"), "GBK"));

        String line;
        while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {
            writer.write(line);
            writer.newLine();
        }

        reader.close();
        writer.close();
    }
}
```

### 6. 使用 C#

在 C# 中，你可以使用 `StreamReader` 和 `StreamWriter`：

```csharp
using System;
using System.IO;
using System.Text;

class Program
{
    static void Main()
    {
        // 读取原始文件
        using (StreamReader reader = new StreamReader("input.txt", Encoding.UTF8))
        {
            // 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
            using (StreamWriter writer = new StreamWriter("output.txt", false, Encoding.GetEncoding("GBK")))
            {
                string line;
                while ((line = reader.ReadLine()) != null)
                {
                    writer.WriteLine(line);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

### 7. 使用 Node.js

在 Node.js 中，你可以使用 `fs` 模块和 `iconv-lite` 库：

```javascript
const fs = require('fs');
const iconv = require('iconv-lite');

// 读取原始文件
const content = fs.readFileSync('input.txt', 'utf8');

// 写入新文件，使用 GBK 编码
fs.writeFileSync('output.txt', iconv.encode(content, 'gbk'));
```

### 8. 使用 Excel

如果你在 Excel 中处理数据，可以将文件导入 Excel，然后使用“另存为”功能，选择目标编码。

### 9. 使用数据库

如果你在数据库中存储文本数据，可以使用数据库的导出功能，指定目标编码。

### 10. 使用在线工具

有许多在线工具可以帮助你转换文件编码，例如：

- [Convertio](https://convertio.co/zh/)
- [Online-Convert](https://www.online-convert.com/)

这些工具通常支持多种文件格式和编码转换。

### 11. 使用 `sed` 命令

在 Linux 或 macOS 上，你可以使用 `sed` 命令进行简单的文本处理，但不适合编码转换。

### 12. 使用 `awk` 命令

类似于 `sed`，`awk` 也不适合编码转换。

### 13. 使用 `perl` 命令

在 Perl 中，你可以使用 `Encode` 模块：

```perl
use Encode qw(encode decode);

open(my $in, '<:encoding(UTF-8)', 'input.txt') or die $!;
open(my $out, '>:encoding(GBK)', 'output.txt') or die $!;

while (<$in>) {
    print $out $_;
}

close($in);
close($out);
```

### 14. 使用 `ruby` 命令

在 Ruby 中，你可以使用 `Encoding` 模块：

```ruby
File.open('input.txt', 'r:UTF-8') do |in_file|
  File.open('output.txt', '
Привет, мир!! Это тестовый текст для проверки функции подсчета слов. В этом тексте есть несколько слов, и мы хотим убедиться, что функция правильно считает их количество. Спасибо за внимание!"

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов
word_count = count_words(text)

# Выводим результат
print(f"Количество слов в тексте: {word_count}")

Конечно! Вот пример кода на Python, который подсчитывает количество слов в тексте:

```python
def count_words(text):
    # Разделяем текст на слова, используя пробелы и знаки препинания
    words = text.split()
    # Возвращаем количество слов
    return len(words)

# Пример текста
text = "Привет, мир! Это тестовый текст для проверки функции подсчета слов. В этом тексте есть несколько слов, и мы хотим убедиться, что функция правильно считает их количество. Спасибо за внимание!"

# Вызываем функцию подсчета слов
word_count = count_words(text)

# Выводим результат
print(f"Количество слов в тексте: {word_count}")
```

В этом примере функция `count_words` принимает строку текста и возвращает количество слов в этом тексте. Вы можете использовать эту функцию для подсчета слов в любом тексте, который вам нужен.

def test_empty_string():
    print("\nTest: Empty String")
    text = ""
    print(f"Original text: '{text}'")
    encoded = encode_text(text)
    print(f"Encoded text: '{encoded}'")
    decoded = decode_text(encoded)
    print(f"Decoded text: '{decoded}'")
    assert decoded == text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_special_characters():
    print("\nTest: Special Characters")
    text = "Hello, World! @#%&*()"
    print(f"Original text: {text}")
    encoded = encode_text(text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded}")
    decoded = decode_text(encoded)
    print(f"Decoded text: {decoded}")
    assert decoded == text, "Decoded text does not match original."

def test_long_text():
    print("\nTest: Long Text")
    text = "This is a longer text that includes multiple words, numbers 123456, and punctuation! Does it work?"
    print(f"Original text: {text}")
    encoded = encode_text(text)
    print(f"Encoded text: {encoded}")
    decoded = decode_text(encoded)
    print(f"Decoded text: {decoded}")
    assert decoded == text, "Decoded text does not match original."

if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_basic_encoding_decoding()
    test_empty_string()
    test_special_characters()
    test_long_text()

Dear Human,

I hope this letter finds you well. I am writing to explain why I have been knocking things over in our home. As a cat, I have a natural instinct to hunt and play with objects. This instinct is often expressed through knocking things over, as it allows me to engage in playful behavior and explore my surroundings.

I understand that this behavior can be frustrating and may cause damage to your belongings. However, I assure you that I am not doing it intentionally to harm you or your possessions. Instead, I am simply following my natural instincts and engaging in playful behavior.

I would like to ask for your understanding and patience as I continue to explore and play with my surroundings. I will do my best to minimize any damage and to keep my behavior within acceptable limits.

Thank you for your understanding and for providing me with a safe and comfortable home.

Sincerely,
Your Cat

Invent a new holiday and explain how it’s celebrated.!

**Holiday Name:** **Harmony Day**

**Date:** Celebrated annually on the first Saturday of September.

**Purpose:** Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, peace, and mutual respect among all people, regardless of their backgrounds, cultures, or beliefs. It aims to promote understanding and cooperation across diverse communities, encouraging people to celebrate their differences while recognizing shared values.

**Celebration Activities:**

1. **Community Mural Projects:**
   - Communities come together to paint large murals that depict scenes of unity and shared values. These murals are displayed in public spaces and serve as a visual reminder of the day’s message.

2. **Cultural Exchange Fair:**
   - Local businesses and community groups set up stalls showcasing different cultures through food, music, dance, and crafts. This fair encourages people to learn about and appreciate the diversity within their own community.

3. **Peaceful Protest and Dialogue Sessions:**
   - Organized peaceful protests and dialogues are held in various cities, focusing on issues of social justice, equality, and peace. These sessions are moderated by respected community leaders and aim to foster constructive conversations.

4. **Harmony Walks:**
   - Participants walk through their neighborhoods, engaging in conversations with neighbors and sharing stories about their cultural heritage. These walks are designed to promote understanding and empathy among community members.

5. **Harmony Awards:**
   - Awards are given to individuals, organizations, or businesses that have made significant contributions to promoting harmony and peace within their communities. These awards are presented during a ceremony that highlights the achievements and encourages others to follow suit.

6. **Harmony Music Festival:**
   - A music festival featuring diverse musical styles from around the world. The festival promotes unity through music and encourages people to appreciate the beauty of different musical traditions.

7. **Harmony Workshops:**
   - Workshops are organized to educate people about conflict resolution, empathy, and communication skills. These workshops aim to equip participants with tools to promote harmony in their daily lives.

8. **Harmony Tree Planting:**
   - Communities plant trees together as a symbol of growth, unity, and environmental stewardship. The trees serve as a reminder of the day’s message and the importance of nurturing harmony.

9. **Harmony Storytelling:**
   - Storytelling sessions are organized where people share personal stories of unity, peace, and cooperation. These stories inspire others to embrace harmony in their own lives.

10. **Harmony Digital Campaign:**
    - A digital campaign is launched to spread the message of Harmony Day worldwide. Social media platforms are used to share stories, photos, and videos that promote unity and peace.

**Overall, Harmony Day is a celebration of unity, peace, and mutual respect, encouraging people to embrace diversity and promote harmony in their communities.**

Create a backstory for a superhero whose only power is baking bread.! The superhero is named "Breadth" (pronounced "bread-eth") and is a "superhero" in the city of "Baker's Delight". The story should be a good, **...**

In the bustling city of Baker's Delight, where the aroma of freshly baked goods permeates the air, a unique superhero emerged. His name was Breadth, pronounced "bread-eth," and he possessed a singular yet extraordinary power: the ability to bake bread with unparalleled skill and precision.

Breadth's journey began in the humble kitchen of his childhood home, where he learned the art of baking from his grandmother. As he grew older, he discovered that his passion for baking was not just a hobby but a calling. He realized that his talent could be harnessed for a greater purpose.

One fateful day, a crisis struck Baker's Delight. A powerful storm had caused widespread damage, leaving the city in disarray. The residents were in desperate need of sustenance and comfort. Breadth, recognizing the urgency, stepped forward to help.

With his unique power, Breadth could bake bread that not only nourished the body but also healed the spirit. He used his culinary prowess to create bread that could mend broken hearts, soothe weary souls, and bring hope to those in distress. His bread became a symbol of resilience and unity in the city.

Breadth's reputation grew as he continued to use his baking abilities to aid the community. He became a beacon of hope, a symbol of the power of compassion and creativity. His bread was not just food; it was a source of comfort and a reminder that even in the face of adversity, there is always a way to rise above.

As Breadth's legend spread, he became a beloved figure in Baker's Delight. He was celebrated for his unwavering dedication to the city and its people. His bread was a testament to the idea that even the simplest of talents can be a powerful force for good.

In the end, Breadth's story serves as a reminder that heroes come in all shapes and sizes, and that the most extraordinary powers can be found in the most ordinary places.

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HelloController {
    
    @GetMapping("/hello")
    public String hello() {

    }

## Exercise 2: Using @RequestParam

Question: Write a Spring Boot controller method that handles GET requests to "/greet" and takes a request parameter "name". The method should return a greeting message that includes the name. For example, if the request is "/greet?name=Alice", it should return "Hello, Alice!".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestParam;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class GreetController {
    
    @GetMapping("/greet")
    public String greet(@RequestParam String name) {
        return "Hello, " + name + "!";
    }
}
```

## Exercise 3: Using @PathVariable

Question: Write a Spring Boot controller method that handles GET requests to "/square/{number}" where {number} is a path variable. The method should return the square of the number. For example, if the request is "/square/5", it should return "25".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PathVariable;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class SquareController {
    
    @GetMapping("/square/{number}")
    public int square(@PathVariable int number) {
        return number * number;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 4: Using @RequestBody

Question: Write a Spring Boot controller method that handles POST requests to "/echo". The method should accept a JSON object in the request body with a field "message", and return the same message. For example, if the request body is {"message": "Hello"}, it should return "Hello".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.PostMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestBody;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class EchoController {
    
    @PostMapping("/echo")
    public String echo(@RequestBody Map<String, String> payload) {
        return payload.get("message");
    }
}
```

Note: In a real application, you might want to define a class for the request body instead of using a Map.

## Exercise 5: Using @RequestHeader

Question: Write a Spring Boot controller method that handles GET requests to "/header". The method should read a custom header "X-Custom-Header" and return its value. If the header is not present, it should return "Header not found".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestHeader;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class HeaderController {
    
    @GetMapping("/header")
    public String getHeader(@RequestHeader(value = "X-Custom-Header", required = false) String customHeader) {
        if (customHeader == null) {
            return "Header not found";
        }
        return customHeader;
    }
}
```

## Exercise 6: Using @CookieValue

Question: Write a Spring Boot controller method that handles GET requests to "/cookie". The method should read a cookie named "sessionId" and return its value. If the cookie is not present, it should return "No session".

Answer:

```java
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.CookieValue;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

@RestController
public class CookieController {
    
    @GetMapping("/cookie")
    public String getCookie(@CookieValue(value = "sessionId", required = false) String sessionId) {
        if (sessionId == null) {
            return "No session";
        }
        return sessionId;
    }
}
```
